To Our Shareholders: This notice and accompanying information statement (the "Information Statement") is being distributed to the holders of record, as of the close of business on October 20, 2026 (the "Record Date"), of the Class A common stock and the Class B common stock of Meta Platforms, Inc. (the "Company," "Meta," "we," "us," or "our"). The purpose of the Information Statement is to inform you of action taken by holders of a majority of the voting power of the outstanding shares of capital stock of the Company to (i) amend and restate the Company's Amended and Restated Certificate of Incorporation (the "Charter"), to be effective as of January 19, 2027 (the "Charter Effective Date"), to, among other things, establish a new class of capital stock, which will be known as Class C common stock (as so amended and restated, the "Restated Charter"), and (ii) amend and restate the Meta Platforms, Inc. 2025 Equity Incentive Plan (the "2025 Plan") to, among other things, provide that shares available for issuance thereunder can consist of either shares of our Class A common stock or shares of our new Class C common stock, as further described in this Information Statement (the "Equity Plan Amendment"). On the Record Date, the Consenting Shareholders (as defined below) delivered to the Company a written consent (the "Written Consent") approving the Restated Charter and the Equity Plan Amendment. The "Consenting Shareholders" are, collectively, CZI Holdings, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings II, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings III, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings IV, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings V, LLC, and Chan Zuckerberg Holdings VI, LLC. As of the close of business on the Record Date, the Consenting Shareholders together owned 315,826,951 shares of the Company's Class B common stock, representing approximately 56.1% of the voting power of the outstanding shares of capital stock of the Company, on a combined basis. Our board of directors (the "Board") has (i) adopted and declared advisable, based on the unanimous recommendation of a special committee consisting of disinterested and independent directors established to review such transaction (the "Special Committee"), the Restated Charter, which adjusts our capital structure by establishing a new class of capital stock, which will be known as Class C common stock (the "Recapitalization"), and (ii) declared a dividend of one share of Class C common stock for each outstanding share of Class A common stock and Class B common stock (the "Dividend"), contingent upon the filing and effectiveness of the Restated Charter. Based on the recommendation of the Special Committee and as further described below, our Board believes that the Recapitalization and Dividend are in the best interests of the Company and our shareholders in order to, among other things, (i) create a publicly traded, non-voting equity security that the Company may use for capital raising, employee compensation, potential acquisitions, and other general corporate purposes, in each case, without diluting the voting power of the existing Class A and Class B common stock; (ii) establish a deep and liquid trading market for Class C common stock designed to minimize any trading-value discount relative to the Class A common stock; (iii) in addition to the initial number of shares of Class C common stock that are freely transferable immediately following the Dividend, ensure that Mr. Zuckerberg's ability to freely transfer his economic interest in his remaining shares of Class C common stock from the Dividend is permitted only in compliance with the Founder Alignment Agreement (as defined below); (iv) ensure that future dividends of Class C common stock pursuant to the Founder Alignment Agreement are subject to the Company's achievement of extraordinary stock price targets and certain other conditions in the Founder Alignment Agreement; and (v) continue to align with the determinations historically made by the Board, acting through its Compensation, Nominating & Governance Committee, as disclosed in the Company's annual proxy statements, that Mr. Zuckerberg's equity ownership sufficiently aligns his interests with those of the Company's shareholders such that no cash compensation or equity or equity-based awards to him are warranted (and, by not having to establish any such cash compensation or grant any such equity or equity-based awards, accrue significant benefit to the Company's shareholders by avoiding the compensation expense and impact on the Company's stock price that any such cash compensation program or equity or equity-based grants to Mr. Zuckerberg would have imposed on the Company's shareholders). The Company also expects to conduct offerings of Class C common stock from time to time in the future. In order to establish the Class C common stock, it is necessary to amend and restate the Charter. The Board has also adopted and declared advisable the Equity Plan Amendment to, among other things, include shares of Class C common stock for issuance thereunder. The Equity Plan Amendment will be effective as of, and is conditioned upon, the filing and 2 effectiveness of the Restated Charter. The Restated Charter and the Equity Plan Amendment were approved by the Consenting Shareholders on October 20, 2026. Following the unanimous recommendation of the Special Committee, the Board approved, declared advisable, and recommended the Restated Charter and the Equity Plan Amendment for approval by the Company's shareholders, and the Consenting Shareholders approved the Restated Charter and the Equity Plan Amendment, in each case, pursuant to the General Corporation Law of the State of Delaware (the "DGCL"), the Charter, and the Company's Amended and Restated Bylaws (the "Bylaws"). A copy of the Restated Charter showing the changes from the Charter, with deleted text shown in strikethrough and added or moved text shown as underlined, is attached as Annex A to the Information Statement. A copy of the Equity Plan Amendment showing the changes from the 2025 Plan, with deleted text shown in strikethrough and added or moved text shown as underlined, is attached as Annex B to the Information Statement. In connection with the Recapitalization, Mr. Zuckerberg entered into the Founder Alignment Agreement with the Company. The Founder Alignment Agreement governs dispositions of shares of Class C common stock by Mr. Zuckerberg and the other members of the Holder Group (as defined below) following the Dividend, as well as certain other conditions. The Founder Alignment Agreement also governs the timing of future dividends of shares of Class C common stock, subject to legally available funds. Mr. Zuckerberg's ability to sell his shares of Class C common stock received in the Dividend is subject to the terms of the Founder Alignment Agreement. A copy of the Founder Alignment Agreement is attached as Annex C to the Information Statement. The Written Consent constitutes the only shareholder approval required to approve the Restated Charter and the Equity Plan Amendment under the DGCL, the Charter, and the Bylaws. The Board is not soliciting your proxy or consent in connection with the Restated Charter and the Equity Plan Amendment and no proxies or consents are being requested from shareholders. The Information Statement is being furnished only to (1) inform the Company's shareholders of the actions described above before they take effect in accordance with Rule 14c-2 promulgated under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934, as amended, and (2) provide the notice required under Section 228(e) of the DGCL. The Company filed the Restated Charter with the Secretary of State of the State of Delaware on October 21, 2026, but the Restated Charter will not be effective until the Charter Effective Date. The Equity Plan Amendment also will be effective upon the Charter Effective Date. This Notice of Internet Availability of Information Statement is being distributed and made available on or about November 2, 2026 to shareholders of record as of the Record Date. This Information Statement does not constitute an offer to sell, or an invitation on our behalf, or a solicitation of an offer to buy, any of the equity securities described herein, including the Class C common stock, and may not be used for or in connection with an offer or solicitation by anyone, in any jurisdiction in which such an offer or solicitation is not authorized or to any person to whom it is unlawful to make such an offer or solicitation. PURPOSE OF INFORMATION STATEMENT This Information Statement (this "Information Statement") advises shareholders of Meta Platforms, Inc. (the "Company," "Meta," "we," "us," or "our") of action taken by holders of a majority of the voting power of the outstanding shares of capital stock of the Company to (i) amend and restate the Company's Amended and Restated Certificate of Incorporation (the "Charter"), to be effective as of January 19, 2027 (the "Charter Effective Date"), to, among other things, establish a new class of capital stock, which will be known as Class C common stock (as so amended and restated, the "Restated Charter"), and (ii) amend and restate the Meta Platforms, Inc. 2025 Equity Incentive Plan (the "2025 Plan") to, among other things, provide that the shares available for issuance can consist of either shares of our Class A common stock or shares of our new Class C common stock, as further described in this Information Statement (the "Equity Plan Amendment"). On October 19, 2026, based on the unanimous recommendation of a special committee consisting of disinterested and independent directors established to review such transaction (the "Special Committee"), the board of directors of the Company (the "Board") approved, declared advisable, and recommended that the Company's shareholders approve the Restated Charter and the Equity Plan Amendment. The record date for the shareholder approval was October 20, 2026 (the "Record Date"). On the Record Date, the Consenting Shareholders (as defined below) delivered to the Company a written consent (the "Written Consent") approving the Restated Charter and the Equity Plan Amendment. The "Consenting Shareholders" are, collectively, CZI Holdings, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings II, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings III, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings IV, LLC, Chan Zuckerberg Holdings V, LLC, and Chan Zuckerberg Holdings VI, LLC. Attached as Annex A to this Information Statement is a copy of the Restated Charter showing the changes from the Charter, with deleted text shown in strikethrough and added or moved text shown as underlined. Attached as Annex B to this Information Statement is a copy of the Equity Plan Amendment showing the changes from the 2025 Plan, with deleted text shown in strikethrough and added or moved text shown as underlined. This Information Statement is being furnished to (1) inform the Company's shareholders of the actions described herein before they take effect in accordance with Rule 14c-2 promulgated under the Securities Exchange Act of 1934, as amended (the "Exchange Act"), and (2) provide the notice required under Section 228(e) of the General Corporation Law of the State of Delaware (the "DGCL"). The Company filed the Restated Charter with the Secretary of State of the State of Delaware on October 21, 2026, but the Restated Charter will not be effective until the Charter Effective Date. The Equity Plan Amendment also will be effective upon the Charter Effective Date. This Information Statement is being distributed and made available on or about November 2, 2026 to shareholders of record as of the Record Date. 4 GENERAL OVERVIEW OF ACTION After receiving the unanimous recommendation of the Special Committee, our Board has determined that it is advisable and in the best interest of the Company and our shareholders to adjust our capital structure by establishing a new class of capital stock, which will be known as Class C common stock (the "Recapitalization") and declare a dividend of one share of Class C common stock for each outstanding share of Class A common stock and Class B common stock. The Class C common stock will be available for use for capital raising, employee compensation, potential acquisitions, and other general corporate purposes. Our Board has declared a dividend of one share of Class C common stock for each outstanding share of Class A common stock and Class B common stock, contingent upon the filing and effectiveness of the Restated Charter (the "Dividend"). The record date and payment date of the Dividend will be determined and approved by the Board by subsequent action, with such payment date to occur no later than the fifth business day following the effectiveness of the Restated Charter, subject to applicable law. Further, in connection with the Recapitalization, the Company has entered into the Founder Alignment Agreement (the "Founder Alignment Agreement") with Mark Zuckerberg, our founder, Chairman, and Chief Executive Officer ("CEO"), as summarized below. Shareholder approval of the Dividend and the Founder Alignment Agreement is not required and was not included in the Written Consent. As more fully explained below, the Recapitalization and the Dividend will not initially affect the relative voting power or economic interest of any shareholder. However, following the payment date of the Dividend, we expect that the market price for the shares of our Class A common stock will reflect the effect of a two-for-one stock split. The shares of our Class A common stock will continue to trade on the Nasdaq Stock Market LLC ("Nasdaq"). We intend to qualify the shares of our Class C common stock for listing on Nasdaq upon their issuance as a result of the Dividend. The Special Committee The Special Committee was established in February 2026 as a committee of our Board, and in March 2026, our Board delegated to the Special Committee the full power and authority of our Board, to the fullest extent permitted under the DGCL, to (i) review, analyze, and evaluate potential modifications to our capital structure, including the ability to trade shares of our Class B common stock and the potential creation of a new class of low- or no-vote common stock to permit us to raise equity capital, (ii) identify and evaluate potential alternatives to any such modification, (iii) make a recommendation to our Board regarding any such modification or any alternative thereto, and (iv) approve or reject any such modification on behalf of our Board. Our Board appointed Ambassador Robert M. Kimmitt, Charles Songhurst, and Tony Xu as members of the Special Committee, and the members of the Special Committee confirmed Ambassador Kimmitt as its Chair. The Special Committee retained Skadden, Arps, Slate, Meagher & Flom LLP as its legal counsel and Moelis & Company LLC as its financial advisor. Our Board determined that each member of the Special Committee was independent of Mr. Zuckerberg and was disinterested with respect to the Recapitalization, and that none of them had any material interest in the Recapitalization. The Special Committee unanimously (i) determined that each of the Restated Charter, the Dividend, and the Founder Alignment Agreement is advisable, fair to, and in the best interests of the Company and our shareholders, including the holders of our Class A common stock, (ii) recommended that our Board approve, adopt, and declare advisable the Restated Charter and thereafter submit it for the written consent of the shareholders entitled to vote thereon, and (iii) recommended that our Board declare and pay the Dividend, effective upon and subject to the effectiveness of the Restated Charter. Founder Alignment Agreement In connection with the Recapitalization and the Dividend, the Company and Mark Zuckerberg have entered into a Founder Alignment Agreement, which limits the ability of Mr. Zuckerberg and certain of his permitted transferees (together with Mr. Zuckerberg, the “Holder Group”) to sell shares of Class C common stock without a corresponding reduction in Mr. Zuckerberg’s voting power, by conditioning sales of such shares on compliance with the Stapling Condition (as described below). Mr. Zuckerberg generally cannot obtain liquidity in a significant majority of the non-voting Class C common stock that he will receive in the Dividend without a corresponding reduction in his voting power. Pursuant to the Founder Alignment Agreement, 20% of the shares of Class C common stock to be issued to the Holder Group in the Dividend will be freely saleable and the other 80% of the shares of Class C common stock will be subject to sale and transfer restrictions (any shares of Class C common stock subject to such sale and transfer restrictions, the “Stapled Class C Shares”). The Holder Group generally may only sell Stapled Class C Shares if, for each such Stapled Class C Share sold, the Holder Group, no later than two business days after the settlement of such sale, sells one share of Class A or Class B common stock, or converts one share of Class B common stock into a share of Class A common stock, or a combination thereof, or applies Stapling Credits (as defined below) (the “Stapling Condition”). However, if immediately prior to such sale the number of Stapled Class C Shares 5 then held by the Holder Group exceeds the sum of the shares of Class A common stock and Class B common stock then held by the Holder Group plus its unapplied Stapling Credits (including as a result of the payment of Additional Dividends (as defined below)) (the “Unstapling Capacity”), the number of shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock that the Holder Group must sell, or the number of shares of Class B common stock that the Holder Group must convert into shares of Class A common stock, or any combination thereof, will be proportionately reduced (rounded up to the nearest whole share) by multiplying it by a fraction equal to the Unstapling Capacity divided by the number of Stapled Class C Shares then held, which fraction shall not be greater than one (the “Unstapling Ratio”). In addition, following the payment of the Dividend, the Holder Group accrues credit for each sale of Class A common stock or Class B common stock, or conversion of Class B common stock into Class A common stock, that is not made in connection with a sale of Stapled Class C Shares (each such credit, as equitably adjusted for stock splits, reverse stock splits, combinations, reclassifications or similar events, a “Stapling Credit”) that may be applied to future sales of Stapled Class C Shares, at a rate equal to the Unstapling Ratio per Stapled Class C Share sold, such that such sales of Stapled Class C Shares are deemed to be in compliance with the Stapling Condition. Stapling Credits do not expire and may be accrued and applied in fractional amounts. At the time the Holder Group no longer holds any shares of Class B common stock, all Stapled Class C Shares then held by the Holder Group will be deemed released from the Stapling Condition and no shares held or acquired thereafter will be subject to the Stapling Condition. If a member of the Holder Group sells Stapled Class C Shares in violation of the Stapling Condition, the Holder Group will be deemed to have irrevocably converted a number of its shares of Class B common stock into Class A common stock equal to the number of Stapled Class C Shares so sold multiplied by the Unstapling Ratio (rounded up to the nearest whole share). The Founder Alignment Agreement provides that, in the case of an inadvertent error with respect to a sale or transfer of Stapled Class C Shares, the Holder Group will have five trading days after the earlier of the Company notifying Mr. Zuckerberg, or Mr. Zuckerberg notifying the Company, of such sale to cure the violation and avoid the automatic conversion of shares of Class B common stock by selling or converting the number of shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock that would have been required for a compliant sale, causing the sale of Stapled Class C Shares to be rescinded or unwound, or designating an equal number of unstapled shares of Class C common stock as Stapled Class C Shares. The Holder Group may transfer shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock or Class C common stock among themselves as permitted by the Restated Charter. In addition, Mr. Zuckerberg may also transfer shares of Class A common stock or Class C common stock to any other person in a charitable or estate planning transfer provided that Mr. Zuckerberg has certified to the Company prior to such transfer its treatment as a permitted transferee and such transferee will thereafter be subject to the terms and conditions of the Founder Alignment Agreement, including the Stapling Condition with respect to any Stapled Class C Shares so transferred. Mr. Zuckerberg has also agreed to cause each member of the Holder Group to comply with the Company’s insider trading policy as in effect from time to time, with respect to the Class A common stock, Class B common stock, Class C common stock and all other Company securities. On the last day of the first full calendar quarter following the payment of the Dividend and on the last day of each calendar quarter thereafter, a number of Stapled Class C Shares then held by the Holder Group equal to 2.5% of the shares of Class C common stock issued to the Holder Group in the Dividend will be released from the Stapling Condition, provided that Mr. Zuckerberg remains employed by the Company on the executive team or as the executive chair of the Board, in either case, on a full-time basis (the “Continued Leadership Condition”) at all times during such calendar quarter. Calendar quarters in which the Continued Leadership Condition is not satisfied at all times do not count toward the release schedule, which need not be satisfied over consecutive quarters, but no shares are forfeited if the Continued Leadership Condition is not met. Upon the thirty-second calendar quarter in which the Continued Leadership Condition has been satisfied at all times, all remaining Stapled Class C Shares then held by the Holder Group will be released from the Stapling Condition. Mr. Zuckerberg has also separately committed, for eight years following the date of the Founder Alignment Agreement, and so long as the Continued Leadership Condition is met, to remain a resident of California or any other jurisdiction in which the Company maintains its corporate headquarters, absent material unforeseen circumstances rendering it prudent or advisable for him to reside elsewhere. Under the Founder Alignment Agreement, our Board has agreed to declare an additional dividend of Class C common stock at a 1:1 ratio for each then outstanding share of Class A common stock, Class B common stock and Class C common stock following the first date on which both of the following conditions have been satisfied (such date, an "Additional Dividend Date," and each such dividend, an "Additional Dividend"): (i) the 30-day volume weighted average price of the Class C common stock reaches a successive doubling, initially 2x, then 4x, then 8x and so on, of the corresponding price of the Class A common stock on September 10, 2026 of $580.60 per share, as equitably adjusted for stock splits, stock dividends, recapitalizations and other equitable adjustments (including with respect to the Dividend) (such event, a "Doubling Event"), and (ii) the compound annualized shareholder return from September 10, 2026, calculated on the basis of the 30-day volume weighted average price and giving effect to the reinvestment of cash dividends and distributions, is no less than 10%. No more than one Additional Dividend may be paid in respect of any single Doubling Event, and no Additional Dividend Date may occur following Mr. Zuckerberg's death. Any shares of Class C common stock issued to Mr. Zuckerberg and the Holder Group in any Additional Dividend will not be subject to the Stapling Condition if, on the Additional Dividend Date, the Continued Leadership Condition is satisfied. If, on the Additional Dividend Date, the Continued Leadership Condition is not satisfied, a number of shares of Class C common stock equal to (i) the total number of shares of Class C common stock payable to the Holder Group in connection with the Additional Dividend multiplied by (ii) a fraction equal to (x) the sum of shares of Class B common stock 6 held by the Holder Group plus the number of Stapled Class C Shares held by the Holder Group divided by (y) the total number of shares of Class A, Class B and Class C common stock held by the Holder Group, in each case of clause (x) and (y), measured as of the last date the Continued Leadership Condition was satisfied (the “Post-Leadership Stapling Number”), shall be deemed Stapled Class C Shares and subject to the Stapling Condition and only released from the Stapling Condition if Mr. Zuckerberg thereafter satisfies the Continued Leadership Condition for at least six consecutive months (or upon the Holder Group no longer holding any shares of Class B common stock). If the Board fails to declare and pay an Additional Dividend within a reasonable time and at a time when payment of such Additional Dividend is permitted by Section 173 of the DGCL, as prescribed by the Founder Alignment Agreement, a number of Stapled Class C Shares then held by the Holder Group equal to the number of shares of Class C common stock the Holder Group would have received in such Additional Dividend (or, if fewer, all remaining Stapled Class C shares) will be released from the Stapling Condition, and the Company shall remain obligated to declare and pay such Additional Dividend. In the event of (i) any merger, consolidation, conversion or other business combination requiring the approval of the holders of our capital stock, whether or not the Company is the surviving entity, or the acquisition of all or substantially all of our assets; (ii) any tender or exchange offer by a third party to acquire a majority of the shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock or Class C common stock; or (iii) any tender or exchange offer by the Company to acquire any such shares, neither Mr. Zuckerberg nor the Holder Group may sell, transfer, exchange or convert any shares in or in connection with that transaction for (a) as to their shares of Class A common stock, an amount per share greater than, or a form of consideration different from that which, the other holders of Class A common stock would receive or may elect to receive, (b) as to their shares of Class B common stock, an amount per share greater than, or a form of consideration different from that which, the other holders of Class B common stock would receive or may elect to receive or (c) as to their shares of Class C common stock, an amount per share greater than, or a form of consideration different from that which, the other holders of Class C common stock would receive or may elect to receive. Further, from and after the payment of the Dividend, in the event the Company, with the approval of the Board, determines to repurchase shares of Class A common stock, the Company and the Board shall, in good faith, use their best efforts to contemporaneously repurchase at least an equal number of shares of Class C common stock on an aggregate basis. Mr. Zuckerberg may terminate the Founder Alignment Agreement if the Restated Charter is not filed with the Secretary of State of the State of Delaware within two business days of the date of the Founder Alignment Agreement or is not effective by January 19, 2027 (or any agreed extension), the Dividend is not paid by the fifth business day following the effectiveness of the Restated Charter (subject to applicable Delaware law and any agreed extension), the Company materially breaches the Founder Alignment Agreement and fails to cure within thirty days of receiving notice of such breach, or a material Company commitment under the Founder Alignment Agreement (or under any provision of the Restated Charter added in connection with the matters contemplated by the Founder Alignment Agreement) ceases to be in full force and effect or is modified without Mr. Zuckerberg’s prior written consent. The Company, with the approval of a committee of the Board consisting of at least two directors, or the audit & privacy committee, in each case with members that the Board has determined to be disinterested with respect to the matters set forth in the Founder Alignment Agreement, may terminate the Founder Alignment Agreement if Mr. Zuckerberg materially breaches the Founder Alignment Agreement and fails to cure within thirty days of receiving notice of such breach, or if a material commitment by Mr. Zuckerberg under the Founder Alignment Agreement (or under any provision of the Restated Charter added in connection with the matters contemplated by the Founder Alignment Agreement) ceases to be in full force and effect or is modified without the Company’s prior written consent. The foregoing description of the Founder Alignment Agreement is qualified by reference to, and should be read in conjunction with, the full text of the Founder Alignment Agreement, which is included as Annex C to this Information Statement. The Special Committee's Reasons for the Recapitalization, the Adoption of the Restated Charter, and the Dividend The Special Committee believes that the Recapitalization, the adoption of the Restated Charter, and the declaration and payment of the Dividend, and entering into the Founder Alignment Agreement with Mr. Zuckerberg in accordance with the terms thereof, are advisable, fair to and in the best interests of the Company and our shareholders in order to (i) create a publicly traded, non-voting equity security that the Company may use for capital raising, employee compensation, potential acquisitions, and other general corporate purposes, in each case, without diluting the voting power of the existing Class A and Class B common stock; (ii) establish a deep and liquid trading market for Class C common stock designed to minimize any trading-value discount relative to the Class A common stock; (iii) in addition to the initial number of shares of Class C common stock that are freely transferable immediately following the Dividend, ensure that Mr. Zuckerberg’s ability to freely transfer his economic interest in his remaining shares of Class C common stock from the Dividend is permitted only in compliance with the Stapling Condition, with a number of shares equal to 2.5% of the shares of Class C common stock issued to Mr. Zuckerberg in the Dividend released from such conditions for each full calendar quarter during which the Continued Leadership Condition is satisfied at all times; (iv) ensure that future dividends of Class C common stock pursuant to the Founder Alignment Agreement are subject to the Company’s achievement of extraordinary stock price targets, and, with respect to Mr. Zuckerberg, subject to the Stapling Condition for a number of shares of Class C common stock equal to the Post-Leadership Stapling Number, unless the Continued Leadership Condition is satisfied as of the date of the event giving rise to such dividend; and (v) continue to align with the determinations historically made by the Board, acting through its Compensation, Nominating & Governance 7 Committee, as disclosed in the Company’s annual proxy statements, that Mr. Zuckerberg’s equity ownership sufficiently aligns his interests with those of the Company’s shareholders such that no cash compensation or equity or equity-based awards to him are warranted (and, by not having to establish any such cash compensation or grant any such equity or equity-based awards, accrue significant benefit to the Company’s shareholders by avoiding the compensation expense and impact on the Company’s stock price that any such cash compensation program or equity or equity-based grants to Mr. Zuckerberg would have imposed on the Company’s shareholders). The Special Committee and our Board (with Mr. Zuckerberg abstaining) approved the Recapitalization, the Dividend and the adoption of the Restated Charter and the Special Committee further approved the Founder Alignment Agreement. The discussion of the information and factors that the Special Committee considered below in making its respective determinations, recommendations, and resolutions is not intended to be exhaustive but includes the material factors considered. In view of the wide variety of factors considered and the complexity of these matters, the Special Committee did not find it useful to, and did not attempt to, quantify, rank, or otherwise assign relative weight to these factors. In addition, the individual members of the Special Committee may have assigned different weights to different factors. At all times, the members of the Special Committee prioritized the best interests of the Company’s Class A shareholders. Provide the Company with Necessary Liquid Capital to Evolve, Grow, and Compete The Special Committee determined that the ability to issue non-voting shares of Class C common stock via the Recapitalization and the Dividend will provide us with the critical ability to raise equity capital to finance our evolution and growth, and help us remain a leader among fierce competition domestically and from foreign competitors. The scale of investment required to compete, sustain our growth, expand our infrastructure, and pursue new strategic initiatives amidst the dramatic transformation taking place in the tech industry and global economy related to artificial intelligence is unprecedented. The ability to raise equity capital through issuances of Class C common stock will allow us access to the equity markets to meet our capital requirements without diluting the voting power of our current shareholders or affecting Mr. Zuckerberg’s leadership or his current control of the Company (discussed more fully below), which is in the best interests of all shareholders in light of his track record of creating exceptional value for Company shareholders. The Special Committee considered whether the Company could issue Class A common stock in order to raise capital, but Mr. Zuckerberg made it clear during the course of the negotiations with the Special Committee that he would not support a significant equity financing that would have the effect of reducing his voting power in the Company. The Special Committee also considered whether debt financing could be an alternative for the financing needs of the Company but recognized that debt financing alternatives of the magnitude that Company management was considering could have an adverse effect on the Company’s credit ratings and would be a less attractive alternative for the Company. The Special Committee also recognized that timing is important to secure capital before competitors saturate various funding options. Recent significant capital raises at other notable companies, and others soon anticipated to take place, to fund the same AI growth—for example, Alphabet, SpaceX, Anthropic, and OpenAI—have resulted in an increasingly challenging environment for raising capital. The Special Committee considered that, while the Company presently has sufficient capital resources for its current needs, in order to finance its funding needs going forward it would be prudent for the Company to do so before even more companies have absorbed available capital in the market. In addition, management had conveyed to the Special Committee a desire for the Class C common stock to be available for use as early as the end of 2026. The Special Committee determined that it is preferable to implement the Recapitalization and Dividend now, so that we are positioned to act with speed and flexibility when capital needs or strategic opportunities arise and so that, with the Dividend, there will be a liquid market for the Class C common stock. The Company expects to use shares of Class C common stock to conduct equity offerings from time to time in the future, subject to market conditions. We are at a significant inflection point in our industry where there is rapid technological advance driven by the growth of AI and concomitant enormous need for capital. The Special Committee recognized that this inflection point represents an unprecedented market opportunity for the Company to grow and create value for all shareholders. Establishing the Class C common stock now will make the Class C common stock a viable currency for financings, potential acquisitions and employee compensation in order to pursue that market opportunity. We operate in a highly competitive industry that is subject to rapid and often disruptive technological change, driven by both new competitors and established companies investing in leading-edge technologies. Establishing the availability of Class C common stock now to be used for future equity capital raises will allow us to remain at the forefront of our industry to the benefit of all shareholders of the Company. In addition, the non-voting nature of the Class C common stock will allow the Company to pursue equity capital raises with sovereign financial institutions in light of U.S. foreign direct investment regulatory requirements. Further, it is critical that, along with the establishment of Class C common stock, the Company issue an initial Dividend to create a liquid market for the Class C common stock. The Special Committee considered it important, through the Dividend, to establish a deep and liquid trading market for Class C common stock to minimize any trading-value discount relative to the Class A common stock and maximize the price for which Class C common stock could be sold in the market. Without creating such liquidity, it would be very difficult to utilize the Class C common stock for capital raises without the Class C common stock potentially suffering a very significant discount relative to the trading price of Class A common stock, which could significantly 8 reduce the amount of capital the Company could raise and have the effect of diluting all shareholders of the Company or could require us to issue more Class C common stock in order to raise the same amount of capital. Continue Mr. Zuckerberg’s Long-Term Value-Maximization for All Shareholders Since our Company’s formation, our management team has focused on and prioritized long-term goals. Over fourteen years since our IPO, the Company’s stock price has increased over 1,700%, from $38.23 per share to $694.25 per share as of the close of market on October 20, 2026. By contrast, over the same period, the S&P 500 increased approximately 495%. The Special Committee recognizes that this success is in large part due to Mr. Zuckerberg’s strong leadership, unique and creative vision, and exceptional management abilities. The Special Committee wants the Company and Mr. Zuckerberg to continue innovating, emphasizing long-term growth and competing with other large-scale players who have already committed to unprecedented levels of capital expenditures in connection with the development of artificial intelligence. The Special Committee recognized that the Company’s track record of huge value creation for the Company’s shareholders is inseparable from Mr. Zuckerberg’s continued involvement in and direction of the business, and that he is the leader best suited to shepherd the Company into its next era and beyond, and that retaining his leadership and guidance of the Company was therefore of utmost importance. Having weighed all relevant considerations, the Special Committee concluded that Mr. Zuckerberg’s continued leadership role in our Company, and his continuing presence at the Company’s headquarters in California, is critical at this current inflection point for the Company and the tech industry in order to continue to provide value-maximization for all shareholders, and that those benefits substantially outweigh any countervailing considerations. Our current corporate governance structure, which uses a dual-class stock structure, allows Mr. Zuckerberg to determine the outcome of substantially all matters submitted to a vote of our shareholders, including the election of directors, amendments to our certificate of incorporation, and matters involving mergers, acquisitions, and other transactions resulting in a change of control of our company. The Recapitalization will facilitate the continuation of our current corporate governance structure, which the Special Committee believes has served us well by enabling us to continue to focus on pursuing our mission and serving the best interests of all of our shareholders by allowing Mr. Zuckerberg to make innovative and smart investments to grow the Company over a long-term horizon. Even though Mr. Zuckerberg has voting control of the Company, all of the directors engage meaningfully in Company matters, express their candid views with Mr. Zuckerberg, and do not rubber stamp decisions. As set forth in greater detail below, the Recapitalization and Dividend will allow the Company to raise critical equity capital without impacting Mr. Zuckerberg’s voting power and the unique and creative vision and exceptional management abilities which helped to deliver the long-term value as noted above. Further, the Dividend will allow Mr. Zuckerberg to immediately sell or transfer up to 20% of the shares of Class C common stock that he receives in the Dividend, representing 10% of his overall economic stake in the Company, for any reason, including so that he may pay his potential tax liability under California Proposition 40, if enacted into law, without affecting his majority voting control. The Recapitalization will also allow us to make stock-based acquisitions and to continue to grant equity awards to our employees without affecting Mr. Zuckerberg’s majority voting control. Mr. Zuckerberg Is Not in Jeopardy of Losing Voting Control but for the Class C Share Issuance As discussed herein, the Special Committee determined that it is important for Mr. Zuckerberg to retain voting control. However, the Special Committee also noted that, unlike situations where a recapitalization may be a remedy to avoid a founder going below 50% voting power, Mr. Zuckerberg is not on the verge of losing voting control. As of October 1, 2026, he held approximately 60.6% voting power over the Company, so the Class C common stock and Dividend do not save Mr. Zuckerberg from losing voting control. The Special Committee considered the following factors: (i) Mr. Zuckerberg is not close to going below 50% voting control currently; (ii) the currently foreseeable reasons why he might approach 50% in the near- to medium-term would be if he were required to pay the California wealth tax (which has the most significant impact because it would require him to sell shares, or at least borrow against them) and the impact of equity financings if they were done with Class A common stock (which is less impactful because it is simply dilution through primary share issuance); (iii) stock repurchases and sales of Class B common stock by other holders of such stock in the past underscored that a dilution of voting control over time through the natural course was not necessarily inevitable; and (iv) Mr. Zuckerberg made clear that he does not support any financing, or other actions, that would result in his loss of voting control. Ensure That Mr. Zuckerberg’s Economic and Voting Control Do Not Significantly Diverge Although the Special Committee determined that it is important that Mr. Zuckerberg’s voting control is not diluted, the Special Committee also concluded that it is beneficial for Class A shareholders that Mr. Zuckerberg’s economic interests and voting control do not significantly diverge, it being understood that the Special Committee desired both to incentivize Mr. Zuckerberg’s continued leadership of the Company combined with extraordinary stock performance of the Company that 9 benefits all shareholders. Therefore, the Special Committee negotiated for and obtained Mr. Zuckerberg’s agreement that 80% of the shares of Class C common stock that he receives in the Dividend be subject to the Stapling Condition. Specifically, Mr. Zuckerberg may sell the remaining 80% of his shares of Class C common stock that he receives in the Dividend only if, no later than two business days after the settlement of such sale, he (A) also sells shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock, or converts shares of Class B common stock into Class A common stock, or a combination thereof, equal in the aggregate to the product of (x) the number of Stapled Class C Shares sold and (y) the Unstapling Ratio or (B) applies a number of Stapling Credits to the sale equal to the product of (x) the number of Stapled Class C Shares sold and (y) the Unstapling Ratio. Further, any Stapled Class C Shares that are sold not in compliance with the foregoing agreement will, in accordance with the terms of the Founder Alignment Agreement, result in the conversion of a number of shares of Class B common stock, equal to the product of (i) the number of Stapled Class C Shares sold in violation of the Stapling Condition and (ii) the Unstapling Ratio, into shares of Class A common stock. This arrangement is subject to Mr. Zuckerberg’s further ability to have a small amount of the 80% be freed from the Stapling Condition each quarter during which he continues to satisfy the Continued Leadership Condition over 32 quarters (the “Release Period”), discussed more fully below. This is to ensure Mr. Zuckerberg’s commitment to continue to lead the Company. The Issuance of Additional Class C Dividends Is Subject to Future Company Success Under Mr. Zuckerberg As described above, the Dividend, with a 1:1 ratio, will allow Mr. Zuckerberg to sell, over the Release Period and subject to the Stapling Condition, up to one-half of his economic stake in the Company through the sale of his new shares of Class C common stock without reducing his voting control. Even if Mr. Zuckerberg were ultimately to sell all of the shares of Class C common stock he receives in the Dividend, based on a stock price of $725.93 per share of Class A common stock as of October 1, 2026 and his stock holdings as of such date, he would still have an approximately $124 billion economic interest in the Company at the end of the Release Period. In order for Mr. Zuckerberg to achieve additional liquidity for and transfer economic interests of the other half of his economic interest in the Company without reducing his voting interests, the Special Committee negotiated for certain stock-performance parameters that to date have not existed. Under the Founder Alignment Agreement, additional Class C dividends after the Dividend would be payable pro rata to all shareholders only upon the Company’s achievement of such stock performance parameters, and Mr. Zuckerberg’s Class C shares received in any such dividend would be free of the Stapling Condition only if he has satisfied the Continued Leadership Condition, both of which directly benefit the Class A shareholders. Specifically, following the first date on which both (i) the 30-day volume weighted average price of the Class C common stock reaches a successive doubling, initially 2x, then 4x, then 8x and so on, of the corresponding price of the Class A common stock on September 10, 2026 of $580.60 per share, as equitably adjusted for stock splits, stock dividends, recapitalizations and other equitable adjustments (including with respect to the Dividend) and (ii) the compound annualized shareholder return from September 10, 2026, calculated on the basis of the 30-day volume weighted average price and giving effect to the reinvestment of cash dividends and distributions, is no less than 10% (whether or not both conditions are satisfied at the same time), the Board will, subject to legally available funds, declare an Additional Dividend on all shares of Class A, Class B, and Class C common stock then-outstanding, not just Mr. Zuckerberg’s. No more than one such dividend may be paid in respect of any single doubling event, and no Additional Dividend will be payable following Mr. Zuckerberg's death. Shares of Class C common stock received by Mr. Zuckerberg in any such Additional Dividend will be free of the Stapling Condition only if the Continued Leadership Condition is satisfied on the Additional Dividend Date; if it is not, a number of shares of Class C common stock received in respect of any such Additional Dividend equaling the Post-Leadership Stapling Number will be subject to the Stapling Condition unless the Continued Leadership Condition is subsequently satisfied for a period of at least six consecutive months. Such extraordinary stock performance is directly beneficial to all Class A shareholders. Ensure That Mr. Zuckerberg Remains in a Leadership Role at the Company’s Headquarters If California’s Proposition 40, a statewide ballot measure on the November 3, 2026 general election ballot and commonly referred to as the “California wealth tax,” is enacted as law in California, it is expected that Mr. Zuckerberg could become liable for a significant, multi-billion dollar tax payment based on his ownership of stock in the Company. In light of this potential tax exposure, Mr. Zuckerberg informed the Special Committee that he was evaluating relocating his primary residence outside California and that steps toward any such relocation, including any listing of his residential real estate in California on the market, would be public and inevitably noted and commented upon by Company employees and, accordingly, raise questions for management of the Company going forward. The Special Committee understood that any such step could be the first in a process to establish residence outside California. The Special Committee determined that Mr. Zuckerberg has been crucial to the Company’s growth since its inception (discussed more fully above) and is best-positioned, during this pivotal time, to ensure the Company’s continued success and to take advantage of the tremendous market opportunity that the Special Committee believed is available to the Company. The Special Committee further believed it is critically important to keep Mr. Zuckerberg in a leadership position with our Company and that he continue to reside in and manage the Company from our headquarters in California to ensure his continued ability to effectively drive our performance during this era of unprecedented challenge and competition in our industry. The Special Committee was mindful about the difficulties the Company faced during the COVID-19 pandemic when Mr. Zuckerberg could not be physically present with the Company’s management team and did not want the Company to face similar challenges going forward. The Special Committee decided 10 that the Recapitalization and Dividend are appropriate to ensure that Mr. Zuckerberg remains in a leadership role, at our headquarters in California, and thus be in the best position to influence our direction in the coming years. Further, the Special Committee determined, in consideration of the dramatic and unprecedented transformation taking place in the tech industry and global economy related to artificial intelligence, that the Recapitalization and Dividend are appropriate to ensure Mr. Zuckerberg remains working closely with Meta Superintelligence Labs at the Company’s headquarters. The Special Committee believes that Mr. Zuckerberg’s talent and leadership have been crucial to our growth, strategy, and stability since our inception, and the Special Committee wants that to continue. Therefore, the Special Committee concluded that it is in the best interests of the Company and Class A shareholders to allow Mr. Zuckerberg to have an additional 2.5% of the shares of Class C common stock from the Dividend be freed from the Stapling Condition every quarter during the Release Period that the Continued Leadership Condition is satisfied. At the conclusion of the Release Period, assuming the Continued Leadership Condition has been satisfied throughout, Mr. Zuckerberg will have the right to sell all of the shares of Class C common stock he received in the Dividend free of the restrictions imposed by the Stapling Condition. If the Continued Leadership Condition is not satisfied at all times during a given quarter, no release will occur for that quarter and the shares not released during such quarter will remain subject to the Stapling Condition until the thirty-second release, if there is one. At the time of the thirty-second release, all of the shares of Class C common stock Mr. Zuckerberg received from the Dividend will be free of the Stapling Condition. Again, the goal is to keep Mr. Zuckerberg continuing to lead the Company from California and in close proximity to Meta Superintelligence Labs. In light of that importance, the Special Committee secured Mr. Zuckerberg’s commitment to remain a resident of California, or any other jurisdiction in which the Company maintains its corporate headquarters, for eight years from the date of the Founder Alignment Agreement, for so long as Mr. Zuckerberg satisfies the Continued Leadership Condition, unless there are material unforeseen circumstances that would render it prudent or advisable for Mr. Zuckerberg to reside elsewhere. Mr. Zuckerberg Will Not Receive Additional Compensation as a Result of the Recapitalization Since January 1, 2013, Mr. Zuckerberg has taken a salary of only $1 each year for his service as the Company’s chief executive officer and chairman of the Board. This is extraordinarily different from what his peer (and even non-peer) CEOs are paid. If the California wealth tax is enacted and Mr. Zuckerberg becomes liable for a tax payment, Mr. Zuckerberg would effectively be paying to serve as the Company’s chief executive officer while residing in California. The Dividend is paid pro rata to all shareholders on identical terms. The portion of Mr. Zuckerberg’s Class C common stock that is immediately free of the Stapling Condition provides him a source of liquidity, without reducing his voting interest, from which any such tax liability could be satisfied, mitigating the unintended result that he would be paying to work at the Company while residing in California. Further, at any time since 2013, Mr. Zuckerberg could have sought a compensation package akin to those of his peer CEOs, and the Special Committee considered that any such package would carry substantial compensation expense and could impact the Company’s stock price. Mr. Zuckerberg confirmed to the Special Committee that in light of the Recapitalization, he is not seeking, and the Founder Alignment Agreement does not provide for, any additional compensation, and that his existing $1 annual salary will remain unchanged. Determinations regarding Mr. Zuckerberg’s compensation, if any, remain within the authority of the Board and its Compensation, Nominating & Governance Committee, which historically has determined that Mr. Zuckerberg’s equity ownership sufficiently aligns his interests with those of the Company’s shareholders, and this continues to be the case in light of the Class C dividends contemplated by the Founder Alignment Agreement. In sum, the terms of the Founder Alignment Agreement, including the release of Class C common stock from the Stapling Condition in accordance with its terms, the dividend of additional Class C common stock pursuant to the Company’s stock performance criteria described herein, and Mr. Zuckerberg’s commitment with respect to his residency were, collectively, negotiated by the Special Committee to preserve the alignment of Mr. Zuckerberg’s interests with those of all shareholders through his continued leadership and retained proportionate ownership, rather than through cash compensation or equity or equity-based awards that would carry compensation expense and could have a negative impact on the Company’s stock price. In other words, the Class C dividends impose no incremental cost on the Company, as a compensation package would have done, with the resulting benefit to Class A shareholders. Mitigating Future Potential Voting Dilution for All Shareholders The Special Committee determined that the shares of Class A common stock have been an important tool for equity-based employee compensation, and may be an important form of currency to help us further our strategic initiatives. However, any new issuances of shares of Class A common stock for either of these key purposes will result in dilution to both the economic and voting interests of all our shareholders. The economic dilution can be mitigated by endeavoring to receive value commensurate with the capital stock that we issue, but the voting dilution cannot be mitigated absent a significant use of cash or the creation of a new class of non-voting shares such as the Class C common stock. Not only are the voting interests of all shareholders diluted by the issuance of additional shares of Class A common stock, but issuances of shares of such stock have the additional effect, which the Special Committee believes is a negative one, of making it more likely that Mr. Zuckerberg would lose majority voting control. 11 We currently have several ways to mitigate voting dilution, including with respect to Mr. Zuckerberg’s majority voting control, to the extent that the Board deems it appropriate, including using cash to finance acquisitions, repurchasing shares of Class A common stock in the market, and granting cash-settled equity incentives. Each of these ways of managing voting dilution, however, requires us to expend cash, which in some circumstances may be better used to reinvest in the growth of our business. Moreover, the Company expects to need to raise capital to finance its AI-related growth plan. The ability to issue non-voting shares of Class C common stock will provide the Board with a significant new tool to mitigate voting dilution without requiring us to use cash. The ability to issue non-voting shares of Class C common stock will also allow us to make decisions regarding appropriate capital structure and financing of the Company (including decisions regarding use of debt versus equity) largely without regard to how those decisions may impact the voting power of all Class A shareholders and Mr. Zuckerberg’s voting control, which we believe is a benefit for our shareholders. Disinterested Directors Empowered to Act in the Interest of Our Shareholders The Special Committee, consisting solely of independent and disinterested directors on the Board, negotiated the terms of the Restated Charter and the Founder Alignment Agreement and had full authority to review, approve or reject any potential change to our capital structure. The Special Committee, with the assistance of its independent financial and legal advisors, conducted an in-depth analytical review of the Recapitalization and Dividend and considered a wide range of alternatives, including the possibility of rejecting any proposed change to our current certificate of incorporation. The Special Committee also met regularly over the course of eight months in 2026 and held a total of thirty-three formal meetings with its legal and financial advisors, Skadden and Moelis, respectively, to review and consider the Recapitalization, the Dividend, the Restated Charter, the Founder Alignment Agreement, and the related arrangements with Mr. Zuckerberg. The Special Committee determined that it was important that the negotiated arrangements remain under the purview of disinterested director oversight. Accordingly, any amendments to or waivers of the Founder Alignment Agreement require approval of an authorized committee of at least two directors, or the Audit & Privacy Committee, in each case with members that the Board has determined to be disinterested with respect to the matters set forth in the Founder Alignment Agreement. That continued oversight over the foregoing agreements with Mr. Zuckerberg will help to ensure continued protection of the Class A shareholders. Other Benefits to the Class A Common Shareholders As noted above, holders of shares of Class A common stock and Class B common stock are currently generally entitled to receive equal per share consideration in a merger or similar business combination (unless the only difference is that the consideration to shares of Class B common stock has ten times the voting power of consideration to shares of Class A common stock). However, shares of Class A common stock and Class B common stock may receive differential consideration if the merger or business combination is approved by the affirmative vote of the holders of the outstanding shares of Class A common stock and Class B common stock, each voting separately as a class. As part of the Recapitalization, we would also amend the current certificate of incorporation to provide that, except as provided in the Restated Charter, the holders of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock shall share ratably on a per share basis in connection with any dividends or distributions. Moreover, in the event of any merger or similar business combination, or in the event of a tender or exchange offer by a third party pursuant to an agreement to which we are a party or that we recommend, the holders of shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock will receive the same form of consideration and the same amount of consideration on a per share basis. However, shares of one or two of such classes may receive or have the right to elect to receive different or disproportionate consideration in connection with any such merger, combination or tender or exchange offer if the only difference in the per share consideration is that any securities distributed to the holder of a share of Class B common stock have ten times the voting power of any securities distributed to the holder of a share of Class A common stock and that any securities distributed to the holder of a share of Class C common stock have no voting rights or power. In addition, upon a liquidation, dissolution, or winding up, all shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock will be entitled to receive ratably all of our assets available for distribution to the shareholders, unless disparate or different treatment of each such class with respect to distributions upon any such liquidation, dissolution, or winding up is approved in advance by the affirmative vote (or written consent if action by written consent of shareholders is permitted at such time under the Restated Charter) of the holders of a majority of the outstanding shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock, each voting separately as a class. 12 Collectively, these provisions are intended to ensure that, even as Mr. Zuckerberg retains majority control over our voting interests, our minority shareholders are treated equally on economic matters. The Special Committee Engaged in Robust Negotiations with Mr. Zuckerberg and Secured Meaningful New Commitments from Mr. Zuckerberg Regarding His Control and Leadership The Special Committee members at all times held the view that their job was to do what is in the best interests of all Class A shareholders. In doing so, and in securing the arrangements described in this Information Statement, the Special Committee engaged in robust negotiations with Mr. Zuckerberg. These negotiations were protracted and, at several points, contentious. The Special Committee insisted that it needed meaningful concessions from Mr. Zuckerberg that would benefit Class A shareholders at every step of the negotiation, and it remained prepared to recommend no transaction at all and to dissolve the Special Committee. These firm negotiations resulted in the meaningful new commitments from Mr. Zuckerberg regarding his control and leadership of the Company described above, which the Special Committee believes are in the best interests of the Class A shareholders. Prior to these arrangements, Mr. Zuckerberg did not have any performance-based requirements regarding his sale of economic interest in the Company while retaining disparate voting control. Further, since the Company’s initial public offering, the certificate of incorporation has provided for a dual-class stock structure and allowed Mr. Zuckerberg to transfer shares to family trusts and other controlled entities without his shares of Class B common stock converting into shares of Class A common stock and there are no time-based or other sunset provisions, thereby allowing Mr. Zuckerberg to maintain generational control. The Special Committee initially proposed implementing sunset provisions to the Company’s dual-class stock structure. However, during the course of the negotiations, Mr. Zuckerberg indicated that he would not support a solution that could involve his losing voting control of the Company or the imposition of sunset provisions. In exchange, the Stapling Condition and performance-based requirements provide important limits on his ability to significantly separate his voting and economic interests and, at the same time, create alignment mechanisms tied to extraordinary stock performance that never before existed. The Special Committee also obtained a commitment from Mr. Zuckerberg that he remain in close proximity to Company headquarters, which also did not exist prior to these arrangements. In light of these critical new commitments from Mr. Zuckerberg, coupled with the urgent need to raise capital for the Company’s future, the Special Committee believes that it negotiated the best set of arrangements reasonably available for the benefit of Class A shareholders. Potential Negative Consequences of the Recapitalization Although the Special Committee unanimously determined that the Recapitalization, Dividend, and adoption of the Restated Charter and Founder Alignment Agreement are, collectively, advisable, fair and in the best interests of the Company and Class A shareholders, the Special Committee recognized that proceeding with the Recapitalization and the Dividend involves certain other considerations that, in isolation, some may view as negative. These considerations include, but are not limited to, the following: The Recapitalization and the Dividend Could Prolong the Period of Time During Which Mr. Zuckerberg Can Exercise a Controlling Influence on Most Corporate Matters Mr. Zuckerberg currently has the ability to elect all of our directors and to determine the outcome of most matters submitted for a vote of our shareholders. This will not change following the Recapitalization and Dividend. This concentration of majority voting control with Mr. Zuckerberg limits the ability of our other shareholders (whether they hold shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock) to influence corporate matters. One of the principal purposes of the Recapitalization and Dividend is to create the Class C common stock, which can then be used to further strategic initiatives, such as financings or acquisitions, or issue future equity awards to our service providers. In the past, we generally issued shares of Class A common stock for such purposes, and over time the issuance of additional shares of Class A common stock would result in voting dilution to all of our shareholders, before accounting for any share repurchases. To the extent such dilution persisted, it could eventually result in Mr. Zuckerberg owning less than a majority of our total outstanding voting power, although his current voting interest exceeds 60% and his economic ownership interest in our Company could continue to be substantial. Once Mr. Zuckerberg owns less than a majority of our total outstanding voting power, he would no longer be guaranteed to have the ability to elect all of our directors and to determine the outcome of any matter submitted for a vote of our shareholders. Because the shares of Class C common stock have no voting rights, the issuance of such shares will not result in further voting dilution for Mr. Zuckerberg. In addition, subject to the Stapling Condition, and the release therefrom pursuant to the terms set forth in the Founder Alignment Agreement, Mr. Zuckerberg will ultimately be free to transfer or otherwise dispose of all the shares of Class C common stock that he would receive in the Dividend. Accordingly, in that event, he would be able to achieve substantial liquidity in his holdings without diminishing his voting control. As a result, the issuance of shares of Class C common stock could substantially prolong the duration of Mr. Zuckerberg’s voting control of our Company and his ability to elect all of our directors and to determine the outcome of most matters submitted to a vote of our shareholders. 13 The Use of Shares of Class C Common Stock as Acquisition Currency May Not Allow for Deferred Tax Treatment Throughout our history, we have been an active acquirer of other companies. Occasionally, we have used stock as consideration to fund these acquisitions and may continue to use Class C common stock as potential consideration for future acquisitions. The use of stock as acquisition currency in certain types of acquisition structures has the benefit of deferring U.S. federal income taxes owed by the sellers in connection with such acquisitions until such time as the sellers dispose of the stock received in such acquisitions. However, in order for certain types of acquisition structures to qualify for this type of deferred tax treatment, the stock used as consideration must be “voting stock” within the meaning of Section 368(a) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended (the “Code”). Because the shares of Class C common stock do not have voting rights (except as required by law), they will not qualify as “voting stock” and their use in connection with certain acquisition structures will not result in deferred tax treatment for the sellers in the acquisition. Sellers may have a preference for a transaction in which they can defer taxes owed, in which case we may have to structure the acquisition in a different manner or may be precluded from using shares of Class C common stock to fund the acquisition. Either of these outcomes could reduce the overall utility of the Class C common stock as a means of managing voting dilution in connection with stock-based acquisitions. The Class C Common Stock Could Trade at a Discount The Special Committee believes that a robust and sufficiently liquid market for the Class C common stock will develop following payment of the Dividend. However, even with the 1:1 dividend, the Class C common stock will likely trade at some level of discount to the Class A common stock. Therefore, we may need to issue more shares of Class C common stock for any equity issuance of the Class C common stock than we would if we were to issue shares of Class A common stock, which could be dilutive to all shareholders. The Special Committee considered that the more shares of Class C common stock distributed through a dividend, and therefore the greater liquidity established for Class C common stock, the lower the discount to the Class A common stock was likely to be. However, the Special Committee determined to balance that against distributing additional Class C common stock through a higher dividend ratio with the effect of providing Mr. Zuckerberg more shares of Class C common stock that he would be able to transfer without reducing his voting interest in the Company. The Recapitalization and Dividend Are Not Subject to the Approval of the Holders of Class A Common Stock The Special Committee was aware of and considered that the support of the Class A common shareholders was not required to approve the Recapitalization or Restated Charter because: (1) a separate class vote of the holders of Class A common stock was not required under our current certificate of incorporation; (2) Mr. Zuckerberg, through his ownership of Class B shares, has the ability to unilaterally approve the Recapitalization and Restated Charter on behalf of our shareholders; (3) the fact that the approval of the Recapitalization and the Restated Charter does not require a shareholder class vote under Delaware law or Nasdaq listing rules; and (4) the fact that these proposals were not conditioned upon the vote of a majority of the holders of Class A common stock. Regardless, the Special Committee determined that the Class A common shareholders will benefit from the collective arrangements described in this Information Statement, including the provisions of the Founder Alignment Agreement, the implementation of the Recapitalization, the adoption of the Restated Charter, and issuance of the Dividend by providing all shareholders with additional liquidity without conceding any of their voting rights, among other things. The Special Committee determined that these are all in the long-term best interests of the Class A shareholders because the Special Committee believes that they will grow share value, which is the primary reason Class A shareholders have invested in the Company. The Recapitalization and Dividend May Negatively Affect the Decision of Institutional Investors to Invest in Us, and Could Have Implications for the Inclusion of Shares of Class A Common Stock or Class C Common Stock in Certain Stock Indices The Recapitalization and Dividend may negatively affect the decision by certain institutional investors to purchase or hold shares of Class A or Class C common stock. The holding of low- or non-voting stock, such as our Class A common stock or Class C common stock, may not be permitted by the investment policies of certain institutional investors or may be less attractive to the portfolio managers of certain institutional investors. In addition, significant sales of shares of Class C common stock by investors who receive such shares as part of the Dividend may occur if such investors are unwilling or unable to hold such shares, which could depress trading prices for the Class C common stock, particularly in the period immediately following the Dividend. The Class A common stock is currently included in certain stock indices, and we expect that the managers of investment funds whose trading is tied to those stock indices may rebalance their holdings to reflect the change in the value of the Class A common stock following the Dividend. Whether the Class C common stock is included in stock indices in the future 14 may also affect trading prices for that stock. For example, it is possible that certain stock indices may only include the Class A common stock, which may reduce the liquidity or trading price of the Class C common stock. However, the Special Committee determined to size the Dividend at the 1:1 ratio in order to increase the likelihood that the Class C common stock would be included in relevant stock indices, while at the same time not setting the Dividend ratio at such a level such that Mr. Zuckerberg could have even greater flexibility to sell his economic interest in the Company without reducing his voting rights. Class C Common Stock May Not Be Attractive as Acquisition Currency or for Equity Incentives or May Result in Greater Economic Dilution We may use shares of Class C common stock from time to time as consideration in connection with the acquisition of other companies. It is possible that companies that we are interested in acquiring will not agree to accept shares of Class C common stock because such shares of capital stock carry no voting rights. Companies that we are interested in acquiring may also refuse to accept shares of Class C common stock if such stock trades at a significant discount to the shares of Class A common stock or if the trading market for the shares of Class C common stock is not well developed or suffers from limited liquidity. We may also decide to issue blank check preferred, Class A common stock or Class B common stock in connection with an acquisition for other reasons. In these instances, if we still wanted to pay for the acquisition with stock consideration, we would need to issue shares of blank check preferred stock, Class A common stock or Class B common stock, all of which is likely to result in both economic and voting dilution to all shareholders. In addition, employees and other service providers may not wish to receive shares of Class C common stock as part of our equity-based compensation programs if the shares of Class C common stock trade at a significant discount to the shares of Class A common stock or if the trading market for the shares of Class C common stock is not well-developed. If employees are not adequately incentivized by receiving shares of Class C common stock, then we may need to issue shares of Class A common stock in order to provide sufficient equity incentives or to issue more shares of Class C common stock, which in either case would result in both economic and voting dilution to all shareholders. Further, if the Class C common stock trades at a discount to the Class A common stock, companies that we are interested in acquiring may demand more shares of Class C common stock in exchange for accepting such stock as consideration. If this occurs, then issuances of Class C common stock may ultimately be more economically dilutive to all of our shareholders than issuances of Class A common stock. Potential U.S. Federal Income Tax Consequences We believe that the Class C common stock should not constitute “Section 306 stock” within the meaning of Section 306(c) of the Code. However, if the Class C common stock were determined to constitute Section 306 stock, a shareholder generally would be treated as realizing ordinary income as opposed to capital gain upon certain dispositions (including redemptions) of such Section 306 stock. The rules of Section 306 of the Code are complex, and each shareholder should consult with that shareholder’s own tax advisor regarding the tax consequences of the proposed transactions described in this Information Statement. Litigation Relating to the Recapitalization Following the announcement of the Recapitalization, litigation has been brought in the Delaware Court of Chancery seeking to prevent the Recapitalization and the Dividend, together with other related actions. The litigation generally alleges that the directors breached their fiduciary duties in connection with the Recapitalization and seeks to permanently enjoin the Recapitalization as well as unspecified money damages, costs and attorneys' fees in addition to declaratory and equitable relief. We believe that the Recapitalization and the Dividend are in the best interests of the Company and our shareholders, and intend to vigorously defend against all claims asserted. Background The Company operates in a highly competitive industry that requires significant capital investment to respond to, and stay ahead of, rapid and often disruptive technological change, driven both by new entrants and established companies investing in leading-edge technologies. By late 2025, the scale of investment required for the Company to compete, sustain its growth, expand its infrastructure, and pursue new strategic initiatives amid the transformation taking place in the technology industry and the global economy in connection with artificial intelligence had become unprecedented. As a result, the Company anticipates needing to raise additional capital in the near term, including in the form of equity, to fund its long-term business plans.
