Its principal terms are: an immediate dividend of one share of Class C common stock on each outstanding share of Class A common stock and Class B common stock; Mr. Zuckerberg’s right to sell 20% of the Class C common stock he receives without restriction, with the remainder subject to the Stapling Condition (satisfied by concurrently selling one share of Class A common stock or Class B common stock, or converting one share of Class B common stock into Class A common stock, for each share of Class C common stock sold, and enforced by the automatic conversion of Class B common stock into Class A common stock for any non-compliant transfer); the release from the Stapling Condition of an additional 2.5% of that Class C common stock for each quarter in which Mr. Zuckerberg is employed full-time on the Company’s executive team or as executive chair of the Board, so that all of it is released after 32 such quarters; for so long as he provides such full-time service, a further one-for-one dividend of Class C common stock on all outstanding shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock upon each Doubling Event (measured against the Class A common stock as of September 10, 2026) at which the Company’s internal rate of return on that reference price is at least 10%, with no more than one such dividend per Doubling Event; a requirement that repurchases of Class C common stock equal or exceed repurchases of Class A common stock and that Class C common stock otherwise receive non-disparate economic treatment; Mr. Zuckerberg’s commitment to remain a resident of California, or any other jurisdiction in which the Company maintains its headquarters, for eight years, absent material unforeseen circumstances; and confirmation that his existing compensation arrangement remains unchanged, with the release schedule, the performance dividends and the residency commitment forming part of the overall agreement. Under these terms, every holder of Class A common stock and Class B common stock, including Mr. Zuckerberg, receives the same one-for-one dividend of Class C common stock and participates on identical terms in any additional dividend. However, the 30 Stapling Condition, the service-based release schedule, and the conditions tied to continued full-time service in the Company and the residency commitment apply only to Mr. Zuckerberg. No other shareholder’s Class C common stock will be subject to any sale or transfer restrictions as a condition of this issuance. Mr. Zuckerberg accepted those conditions, and withdrew his request for a performance-based compensation program, in connection with a dividend that is paid to all shareholders on identical terms and at a ratio designed to establish the liquid trading market for Class C common stock that the Company’s management had advised was necessary to achieve the Company’s financing objectives. The Company will thereby obtain the non-voting equity currency the Board began evaluating in February 2026 without the cost of a performance-based compensation program and without any change to Mr. Zuckerberg’s existing compensation arrangement. On September 20, 2026, as authorized by the Special Committee, Special Committee Counsel delivered a draft of the Founder Alignment Agreement to the Company’s management and Founder Counsel. On September 30, 2026, representatives of each of Special Committee Counsel and Founder Counsel, together with representatives of Davis Polk & Wardwell LLP, outside counsel to the Company, held a discussion regarding the draft of the Founder Alignment Agreement. During this discussion, Founder Counsel previewed proposed revisions to the draft. On October 1, 2026, Founder Counsel shared a revised draft of the Founder Alignment Agreement with Special Committee Counsel. On October 5, 2026, the Special Committee held a meeting with Special Committee Counsel and Moelis to discuss the Founder Alignment Agreement. During the meeting, the Special Committee, Special Committee Counsel and Moelis discussed the revised draft of the Founder Alignment Agreement that Founder Counsel shared with the Special Committee and Special Committee Counsel on October 1, 2026. On October 6, 2026, Special Committee Counsel held a meeting with Founder Counsel to discuss the Founder Alignment Agreement. On October 8, 2026, the Special Committee held a meeting with Special Committee Counsel and Moelis to further discuss the Founder Alignment Agreement. At the conclusion of the meeting, the Special Committee authorized Special Committee Counsel to share a further revised draft of the Founder Alignment Agreement with Founder Counsel. Later that same day, Special Committee Counsel shared a revised draft of the Founder Alignment Agreement with Founder Counsel. Between October 10 and October 14, 2026, Special Committee Counsel and Founder Counsel exchanged a number of drafts of the Founder Alignment Agreement and held a number of conversations to discuss the Founder Alignment Agreement. On October 14, 2026, the Special Committee held a meeting with Special Committee Counsel and Moelis at the conclusion of which the Special Committee unanimously approved the Founder Alignment Agreement and the Company’s entry thereto, approved the adoption of the Restated Charter and the Dividend, and recommended that the Board approve and authorize the Restated Charter and the Dividend and recommend that the Company’s shareholders approve the Restated Charter, and that each of the foregoing is in the Company’s and its shareholders' best interests. Description of Capital Stock The following description of our capital stock summarizes certain provisions of our Restated Charter and our Amended and Restated Bylaws (the "Bylaws"). The description is intended as a summary and is qualified in its entirety by reference to the Restated Charter included as Annex A, with deleted text shown in strikethrough and added text shown as underlined. Our authorized capital stock consists of 109,241,000,000 shares, consisting of (i) 5,000,000,000 shares of Class A common stock, $0.000006 par value per share; (ii) 4,141,000,000 shares of Class B common stock, $0.000006 par value per share; (iii) 100,000,000,000 shares of Class C common stock, $0.000006 par value per share; and (iv) 100,000,000 shares of preferred stock, $0.000006 par value per share. The number of authorized shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, or Class C common stock may be increased or decreased (but not below the number of shares thereof then outstanding) by the affirmative vote of the holders of capital stock representing a majority of the voting power of all the then-outstanding shares of capital stock of the corporation entitled to vote thereon, irrespective of the provisions of Section 242(b)(2) of the General Corporation Law. Common Stock Dividend Rights Subject to preferences that may apply to shares of preferred stock outstanding at the time, the holders of outstanding shares of our common stock are entitled to receive dividends out of funds legally available if our Board, in its discretion, 31 determines to issue dividends and only then at the times and in the amounts that our Board may determine. Shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock shall be treated equally, identically and ratably, on a per share basis, with respect to any dividends or distributions as may be declared and paid from time to time by the Board, provided that in the event a dividend is paid in the form of shares of common stock (or rights to acquire such shares), then holders of each class of common stock shall receive shares of their respective class (or rights to acquire such shares, as the case may be), on a per share basis, in an identical number of shares. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the Board may make a disparate dividend or distribution per share of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock if such disparate dividend or distribution is approved in advance by the affirmative vote of our Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock, each voting separately as a class. In addition, whether or not any shares of Class C common stock are then outstanding, the Board may declare a dividend to be paid in the form of Class C common stock (or rights to acquire, or securities convertible into or exchangeable for, such shares, as the case may be) to all holders of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock then outstanding with holders of shares of Class A common stock, Class B common stock, and Class C common stock receiving, on a per share basis, an identical number of shares of Class C common stock (or rights to acquire, or securities convertible into or exchangeable for, such shares, as the case may be). Voting Rights The holders of our Class B common stock are entitled to ten votes per share, holders of our Class A common stock are entitled to one vote per share, and holders of our Class C common stock have no voting rights, except as provided in the Restated Charter or as required by law. The holders of our Class A common stock and Class B common stock vote together as a single class, unless otherwise required by law. Delaware law could require holders of our Class A common stock, our Class B common stock, or our Class C common stock to vote separately as a class in the following circumstances: • if we were to seek to amend our Restated Charter to increase or decrease the par value of a class of stock, then that class would be required to vote separately to approve the proposed amendment; • if we were to seek to amend our Restated Charter in a manner that alters or changes the powers, preferences, or special rights of a class of stock in a manner that affected its holders adversely, then that class would be required to vote separately to approve the proposed amendment; and • pursuant to the Restated Charter, if any or each class desired to approve disparate treatment in the event of any (i) subdivisions, combinations, or reclassifications; (ii) liquidation, dissolution, or winding up; (iii) merger or consolidation; or (iv) third-party tender or exchange offers. Further, any transaction that would result in a change in control of the Company requires the approval of a majority of the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock, voting as a separate class, until such time as shares of our Class B common stock represent less than thirty-five percent of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock. Shareholders do not have the ability to cumulate votes for the election of directors. In the event the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock represent less than a majority of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock, the Restated Charter provides that our board of directors shall become classified, consisting of three classes of approximately equal size, each serving staggered three-year terms. Our directors will be assigned by the then-current board of directors to a class at such time. No Preemptive or Similar Rights Our common stock is not entitled to preemptive rights and is not subject to redemption or sinking fund provisions. Right to Receive Liquidation Distributions Upon our dissolution, liquidation, or winding up, the assets legally available for distribution to our shareholders are distributable ratably among the holders of each class of our common stock, subject to prior satisfaction of all outstanding debt and liabilities and the preferential rights and payment of liquidation preferences, if any, on any outstanding shares of preferred stock. Conversion Shares of our Class A common stock and Class C common stock are not convertible into any other shares of our capital stock. Shares of our Class B common stock are convertible at any time as follows: (1) at the option of the holder, a share of Class B common stock may be converted at any time into one share of Class A common stock or (2) upon the election of the holders of a majority of the then-outstanding shares of Class B common stock, all outstanding shares of Class B common 32 stock may be converted into shares of Class A common stock. In addition, each share of Class B common stock will convert automatically into one share of Class A common stock upon any transfer, whether or not for value, except for certain transfers described in the Restated Charter, including transfers to family members, trusts solely for the benefit of the shareholder or their family members, and partnerships, corporations, and other entities exclusively owned by the shareholder or their family members. Once converted or transferred and converted into Class A common stock, the Class B common stock will not be reissued. Preferred Stock Subject to limitations prescribed by Delaware law, our Board is authorized to issue preferred stock in one or more series, to establish from time to time the number of shares to be included in each series and to fix the designation, powers, preferences, and rights of the shares of each series and any of its qualifications, limitations, or restrictions. Our Board also can increase or decrease the number of shares of any series, but not below the number of shares of that series then outstanding, without any further vote or action by our shareholders. Our Board may authorize the issuance of preferred stock with voting, conversion, or other rights that could adversely affect the voting power or other rights of the holders of the common stock. The issuance of preferred stock, while providing flexibility in connection with possible acquisitions and other corporate purposes, could, among other things, have the effect of delaying, deferring, or preventing a change in control of the Company. Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock On October 29, 2026, we issued Series A depositary shares (the “Depositary Shares”), each representing a 1/20th interest in a share of our 6.50% Series A Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock, par value $0.000006 per share, liquidation preference $1,000.00 per share (the "Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock"). The Series A depositary shares are listed on the Nasdaq Global Select Market under the symbol "METAM." The preferences, limitations, and relative rights of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock are established in a certificate of designations (the "Original Certificate of Designations") and a restated charter certificate of designations (the "Restated Charter Certificate of Designations" and, together with the Original Certificate of Designations, the “Certificates of Designations”) filed with the Secretary of State of the State of Delaware on October 28, 2026. The Original Certificate of Designations became effective upon filing. The Restated Charter Certificate of Designations will become effective and supersede the Original Certificate of Designations on the Charter Effective Date. The Certificates of Designations establish the same preferences, limitations, and relative rights of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock. Upon the effectiveness of the Restated Charter Certificate of Designations, each issued and outstanding share of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock will continue as an issued and outstanding share of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock designated pursuant to the Restated Charter Certificate of Designations. Subject to certain exceptions, including those applicable to the Dividend, under the terms of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock, so long as any share of Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock remains outstanding, no dividend or distribution may be declared or paid on the Class A common stock, the Class C common stock or any other shares of junior stock, and no Class A common stock, Class C common stock or other junior stock or parity stock may be, directly or indirectly, purchased, redeemed, or otherwise acquired for consideration by the Company or any of its subsidiaries unless all accumulated and unpaid dividends for all preceding dividend periods have been declared and paid upon, or a sufficient sum or number of shares of the Class A common stock or Class C common stock, as the case may be, has been set apart for the payment of such dividends upon, all outstanding shares of Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock. Holders of the Depositary Shares will be entitled to a proportional fractional interest in the rights and preferences of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock, including conversion, dividend, liquidation, and voting rights, subject to the provisions of the deposit agreement, dated October 29, 2026, by and among the Company, Computershare Trust Company, N.A. and Computershare Inc., acting jointly as bank depositary (the "Depositary"), and the holders from time to time of the depositary receipts. The Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock will accumulate dividends (which may be paid in cash or, subject to certain limitations, in shares of the Class A common stock or in any combination of cash and shares of the Class A common stock) at a rate per annum equal to 6.50% on the liquidation preference thereof. The liquidation preference of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock is $1,000 per share. Dividends on the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock will be payable when, as and if declared by the Board (or an authorized committee thereof), on February 15, May 15, August 15, and November 15 of each year, beginning on February 15, 2027 and ending on, and including, August 15, 2029. Unless earlier converted, each outstanding share of Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock will automatically convert for settlement on or about August 15, 2029, into between 1.3620 and 1.6000 shares of Class A common stock (and, correspondingly, each Depositary Share will automatically convert into between 0.0681 and 0.0800 shares of Class A common stock), subject to customary anti-dilution adjustments, determined based on the average volume-weighted average price of the Class A common stock over the 20 consecutive trading day period beginning on, and including, the 21st scheduled trading day prior to August 15, 2029 (the "final averaging period"). Notwithstanding the foregoing, upon the issuance of the Class C common stock and satisfaction of certain other conditions, (1) unless earlier converted, each share of Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock (and, correspondingly, each Depositary Share) will be convertible into shares of Class C common stock for settlement on or about August 15, 2029 in a number not less than the then-current minimum conversion rate nor greater than the then-current maximum conversion rate, determined based on the average volume-weighted average price of the Class C common stock 33 during the final averaging period, (2) other than during a fundamental change conversion period (as defined in the Certificates of Designations), at any time after the 50th trading day immediately following the Class C Dividend Trigger Date (as defined in the Certificates of Designations) and prior to the mandatory conversion settlement date, a holder of 20 Depositary Shares may cause the Depositary to convert one share of Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock, on such holder's behalf, into a number of shares of Class C common stock equal to the then-current minimum conversion rate and (3) cumulative dividends with respect to the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock may be paid in cash or, subject to certain limitations, in shares of Class C common stock or in any combination of cash and Class C common stock, at the election of the Company. If a holder of 20 Depositary Shares causes the Depositary to convert one share of Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock, on such holder's behalf, during a specified period beginning on the fundamental change reference date with respect to a fundamental change, the conversion rate will be adjusted under certain circumstances, and such holder will also be entitled to a make-whole dividend amount. In addition, in the event of the Company's voluntary or involuntary liquidation, winding-up or dissolution, each holder of Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock will be entitled to receive an amount per share of Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock equal to the greater of (x) $1,000 per share of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock plus an amount equal to any accumulated and unpaid dividends per share of such Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock to, but excluding, the date fixed for such liquidation, winding-up or dissolution and (y)(i) the per share amount to be paid or distributed to holders of the Class A common stock or Class C common stock, as applicable, then underlying the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock in connection with such voluntary or involuntary liquidation, winding-up or dissolution, multiplied by (ii) the then-current maximum conversion rate of the Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock, to be paid out of the Company's assets available for distribution to the Company's shareholders, after satisfaction of liabilities to the Company's creditors and holders of any senior stock and before any payment or distribution is made to holders of junior stock, including the Class A common stock and the Class C common stock. Anti-Takeover Provisions So long as the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock represent a majority of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock, Mark Zuckerberg, our founder, Chairman, and CEO, will effectively control all matters submitted to our shareholders for a vote, as well as the overall management and direction of the Company, which will have the effect of delaying, deferring, or discouraging another person from acquiring control of the Company. After such time as the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock no longer represent a majority of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock, the provisions of Delaware law and our Restated Charter and Bylaws may have the effect of delaying, deferring, or discouraging another person from acquiring control of the Company. Restated Charter and Bylaws Provisions Our Restated Charter and Bylaws include a number of provisions that may have the effect of deterring hostile takeovers or delaying or preventing changes in control of the Company, even after such time as the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock no longer represent a majority of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock, including the following: • Separate Class B Vote for Certain Transactions . Any transaction that would result in a change in control of the Company requires the approval of a majority of our outstanding shares of Class B common stock voting as a separate class until such time as the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock represent less than thirty-five percent of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock. This provision could delay or prevent the approval of a change in control that might otherwise be approved by a majority of the outstanding shares of our Class A and Class B common stock voting together on a combined basis. • Multi-Class Stock . Our Restated Charter provides for a multi-class common stock structure, which provides Mark Zuckerberg, our founder, Chairman, and CEO, with the ability to control the outcome of matters requiring shareholder approval, even if he owns significantly less than a majority of the shares of our outstanding capital stock, including the election of directors and significant corporate transactions, such as a merger or other sale of the Company or its assets. • Supermajority Approvals . Our Restated Charter and Bylaws currently do not provide that certain amendments to our Restated Charter or Bylaws by shareholders will require the approval of two-thirds of the combined voting power of our then-outstanding shares of capital stock. However, when the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock represent less than a majority of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock, certain amendments to our Restated Charter or Bylaws by shareholders will require the approval of two-thirds of the combined voting power of our then-outstanding shares of capital stock. This will have the effect of making it more difficult to amend our Restated Charter or Bylaws to remove or modify certain provisions. 34 • Board of Directors Vacancies . Our Restated Charter and Bylaws provide that shareholders may fill vacant directorships. When the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock represent less than a majority of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock, our Restated Charter and Bylaws will authorize only our board of directors to fill vacant directorships. In addition, the number of directors constituting our board of directors is set only by resolution adopted by a majority vote of the total number of authorized directors. These provisions restricting the filling of vacancies will prevent a shareholder from increasing the size of our board of directors and gaining control of our board of directors by filling the resulting vacancies with its own nominees. • Classified Board . Our board of directors is not classified. Our Restated Charter and Bylaws provide that when the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock represent less than a majority of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock, our board of directors will be classified into three classes of directors, each of which will hold office for a three-year term. In addition, thereafter, directors may only be removed from the board of directors for cause. The existence of a classified board could delay a successful tender offeror from obtaining majority control of our board of directors, and the prospect of that delay might deter a potential offeror. • Shareholder Action; Special Meeting of Shareholders . Our Restated Charter and Bylaws provide that shareholders will be able to take action by written consent. When the outstanding shares of our Class B common stock represent less than a majority of the combined voting power of our outstanding capital stock, our shareholders will no longer be able to take action by written consent, and will only be able to take action at annual or special meetings of our shareholders. Shareholders will not be permitted to cumulate their votes for the election of directors. Our Restated Charter and Bylaws further provide that special meetings of our shareholders may be called only by a majority of our board of directors, the chairman of our board of directors, our chief executive officer or our president. • Advance Notice Requirements for Shareholder Proposals and Director Nominations . Our Bylaws provide advance notice procedures for shareholders seeking to bring business before our annual meeting of shareholders, or to nominate candidates for election as directors at any meeting of shareholders. Our Bylaws also specify certain requirements regarding the form and content of a shareholder’s notice. These provisions may preclude our shareholders from bringing matters before our annual meeting of shareholders or from making nominations for directors at our meetings of shareholders. • Issuance of Undesignated Preferred Stock . Our board of directors has the authority, without further action by the shareholders, to issue up to 100,000,000 shares of undesignated preferred stock with rights and preferences, including voting rights, designated from time to time by the board of directors. The existence of authorized but unissued shares of preferred stock enables our board of directors to render more difficult or to discourage an attempt to obtain control of us by means of a merger, tender offer, proxy contest, or otherwise. Delaware Law A Delaware corporation may “opt out” of the business combination provisions of Section 203 of the DGCL with an express provision in its original certificate of incorporation or an express provision in its certificate of incorporation or bylaws resulting from a shareholders’ amendment approved by at least a majority of the outstanding voting shares. We have expressly elected not to be governed by the business combination provisions of Section 203 of the DGCL. Choice of Forum Our Restated Charter provides that the Court of Chancery of the State of Delaware is the exclusive forum for any derivative action or proceeding brought on our behalf; any action asserting a breach of fiduciary duty; any action asserting a claim against us arising pursuant to the DGCL, our Restated Charter or Bylaws; or any action asserting a claim against us that is governed by the internal affairs doctrine. Transferability and Listing Like shares of our Class A common stock, shares of our Class C common stock will be freely transferable. See “Certain Other Effects of the Recapitalization – Securities Act of 1933” below. The shares of our Class A common stock are currently listed on the Nasdaq Global Select Market under the symbol “META.” Prior to the date the Dividend is paid, we intend to file appropriate applications and notices with Nasdaq to list the shares of our Class C common stock. The listing of the shares of our Class C common stock on Nasdaq is subject to Nasdaq’s approval of such listing applications and notices, which will be conditioned upon Meta’s satisfaction of certain listing requirements. We believe that we will be able to satisfy these listing requirements upon the payment of the Dividend. Pursuant to the Founder Alignment Agreement, Mr. Zuckerberg and the other members of the Holder Group may sell or transfer up to 20% of the total shares of Class C common stock they receive from the Dividend free of the Stapling Condition, and thereafter may sell or transfer the remainder of the shares they receive in the Dividend subject to the Stapling Condition, 35 and any shares of Class C common stock that are sold not in compliance with the foregoing will result in the conversion of a number of shares of Class B common stock equal to the product of the number of Stapled Class C Shares so sold multiplied by the Unstapling Ratio (rounded up to the nearest whole share) into shares of Class A common stock, subject to a five-trading-day cure period for inadvertent errors, provided , however , that for every quarter during which Mr. Zuckerberg satisfies the Continued Leadership Condition at all times, 2.5% of the shares of Class C common stock he (and the other members of the Holder Group) receives in the Dividend will be released from the Stapling Condition. Accordingly, following 32 quarters where the Continued Leadership Condition is satisfied, Mr. Zuckerberg and the other members of the Holder Group will have the right to sell all of the shares of Class C common stock received in the Dividend free of the Stapling Condition. Pursuant to the Stapling Condition, Mr. Zuckerberg and the other members of the Holder Group may generally only sell or transfer Stapled Class C Shares if, for each such Stapled Class C Share sold, the Holder Group, no later than two business days after the settlement of such sale, sells an equal number of shares of Class A or Class B common stock, or converts an equal number of shares of Class B common stock into shares of Class A common stock, or a combination thereof, or applies an equal number of Stapling Credits to the sale. However, if immediately prior to such sale the number of Stapled Class C Shares then held by the Holder Group exceeds the Unstapling Capacity, the number of shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock that the Holder Group must sell, the number of shares of Class B common stock that the Holder Group must convert into shares of Class A common stock, or the number of Stapling Credits that must be applied to such sale will be proportionately adjusted by multiplying such number by the Unstapling Ratio. Transfer Agent and Registrar The transfer agent and registrar for our common stock is Computershare Trust Company, N.A. Certain Other Effects of the Recapitalization Effect on Relative Voting Power and Equity Interest There will be no effect on the relative voting power or equity interest of each holder of shares of our Class A common stock or Class B common stock. Following the Dividend, holders of shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock who sell their shares of Class C common stock will not lose any voting power, but their relative equity interest in Meta will decrease as a result of such sale. Conversely, shareholders who purchase shares of Class C common stock after the Dividend will increase their relative equity interest in Meta but will not gain any additional voting power (or any voting power, if they do not otherwise own shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock). Effect on Market Price As of the close of business on October 20, 2026, the closing price of a share of our Class A common stock was $694.25 as reported on Nasdaq. We believe that the market price for the shares of Class A common stock will reflect the effect of a two-for-one stock split once the Dividend is paid and, accordingly, the market price of the Class A common stock will decrease by approximately 50%. We expect the market price of shares of Class C common stock to be approximately equal to the market price of shares of Class A common stock (as such price is adjusted as a result of the Dividend). The trading prices for shares of Class A common stock and Class C common stock may be affected by the relative voting rights between these two classes of stock. Because the Class A common stock carries voting rights, it is possible that it could trade at a premium compared to the Class C common stock, which does not carry voting rights. Furthermore, the trading price of shares of Class A common stock and Class C common stock will continue to depend on many factors, including the future performance of Meta, global market conditions, and conditions relating to companies in businesses and industries similar to that of Meta. Accordingly, we cannot predict the prices at which shares of Class A common stock and Class C common stock will trade following the Recapitalization (and, in respect of shares of Class C common stock, following the Dividend), just as we could not predict the price at which shares of Class A common stock would trade absent the Recapitalization and the Dividend. Following the Recapitalization, there will continue to be no trading market for the Class B common stock. Effect on Trading Market and Potential Reduced Relative Liquidity of Shares of Class A Common Stock From time to time we may issue shares of Class C common stock in the future for capital raising, employee compensation, acquisitions, and other general corporate purposes. In addition, pursuant to the terms of the Founder Alignment Agreement, the Board will be required to declare and pay additional dividends of one share of Class C common stock on each outstanding share of Class A common stock, Class B common stock and Class C common stock (each, an "Additional Dividend") upon the occurrence of Doubling Events, provided that the IRR Threshold (as defined in the Founder Alignment Agreement) has been achieved, in each case to the extent permitted by applicable law; provided, further, that no Additional 36 Dividend Date shall occur following Mr. Zuckerberg’s death. It is possible that following the Dividend and any future dividends payable in Class C common stock, some portion of our shareholders will sell their shares of Class C common stock but retain their shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock in order to monetize a portion of their investment in Meta while retaining their relative voting power (subject to the terms of the Founder Alignment Agreement in the case of Mr. Zuckerberg). Any such issuance of additional shares of Class C common stock by Meta or dispositions of shares of Class C common stock by significant or other shareholders may serve to further increase market activity in the shares of Class C common stock relative to the shares of Class A common stock. Effect on Percentage Interest The percentage interest of each shareholder in the total equity of Meta will not be changed by the Recapitalization or the Dividend. Effect on Equity Incentive Plans and Outstanding Equity Awards As described in more detail under the section entitled “ Amendment to the 2025 Plan ” below, the Board and the Consenting Shareholders have approved the Equity Plan Amendment which, among other things, provides that the shares available for issuance pursuant to awards under the 2025 Plan may consist of either shares of Class A common stock or shares of Class C common stock. Upon the effectiveness of the Equity Plan Amendment, the number of shares available for issuance pursuant to awards under the 2025 Plan shall be equal to the number of shares available for future grant and issuance under the 2025 Plan as of the Record Date, plus (i) any shares added to the 2025 Plan prior to the Charter Effective Date pursuant to the Evergreen Feature (as defined below), minus (ii) any shares that are subject to awards granted under the 2025 Plan between the Record Date and the Charter Effective Date, plus (iii) any shares subject to awards previously granted under the 2025 Plan that are outstanding as of the Charter Effective Date, plus (iv) any shares that are subject to awards granted under the 2025 Plan that cease to be subject to such award by forfeiture or otherwise, or that are used or withheld to pay the exercise price of an option or to satisfy the tax withholding obligations related to any award under the 2025 Plan, in each case between the Record Date and the Charter Effective Date, plus (v) any shares that are subject to awards granted under the Company's 2012 Equity Incentive Plan (the "2012 Plan") that cease to be subject to such award by forfeiture or otherwise after the Record Date, and plus (vi) any shares that are subject to awards under the 2012 Plan that are used or withheld to pay the exercise price of an option or to satisfy the tax withholding obligations related to any Award after the Record Date (collectively, the “Plan Pool”). The Equity Plan Amendment does not increase the aggregate number of shares available for issuance under the 2025 Plan. However, as a result of the Dividend, and contingent upon the payment of the Dividend, the Board has approved (i) an equitable adjustment to the Plan Pool, pursuant to the Board’s authority under the 2025 Plan, and (ii) an equitable adjustment to all awards outstanding under the 2025 Plan and the 2012 Plan, pursuant to the Board’s authority thereunder, to provide that upon the exercise or settlement of such award, the applicable holder will be entitled to receive one share of Class C common stock for each share of Class A common stock issuable thereunder, and with respect to any outstanding options, the exercise price will be equitably adjusted. Accounting Matters The par value per share of our shares of Class A common stock and Class B common stock will remain unchanged at $0.000006 per share after the Recapitalization and the Dividend. On the effective date of the Dividend there will be an increase in the "common stock" line item in our consolidated balance sheet equal to the aggregate amount of Class C common stock, par value $0.000006 per share, that is issued, with a corresponding decrease in retained earnings. We will give retroactive effect to prior period share and per share amounts in our consolidated financial statements for the effect of the Recapitalization and the Dividend such that prior periods are comparable to current period presentation. We do not anticipate any other material accounting consequences as a result of the Recapitalization and the Dividend. Effect on Preferred Stock Neither the Restated Charter nor the Dividend will have any effect on the number of authorized shares of our preferred stock or the rights, preferences, and privileges of, and restrictions on, the preferred stock, other than as described under the section entitled " Mandatory Convertible Preferred Stock " above. Certain U.S. Federal Income Tax Consequences THIS SUMMARY IS FOR GENERAL INFORMATION PURPOSES ONLY, AND IS NOT INTENDED TO BE, AND SHOULD NOT BE CONSTRUED TO BE, LEGAL OR TAX ADVICE. YOU ARE URGED TO CONSULT YOUR TAX ADVISOR WITH REGARD TO THE APPLICATION OF THE U.S. FEDERAL INCOME TAX LAWS, AS WELL AS THE APPLICATION OF U.S. NON-INCOME TAX LAWS AND THE LAWS OF ANY STATE, LOCAL, OR FOREIGN TAXING JURISDICTION, IN LIGHT OF YOUR PARTICULAR SITUATION. 37 In general, for U.S. federal income tax purposes: (i) we do not expect to treat either the Recapitalization or the Dividend as taxable to our shareholders and (ii) we expect the Class C common stock to constitute stock that is not "Section 306 stock" within the meaning of Section 306(c) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended (the "Code"). Assuming this treatment applies, (i) the tax basis of each share of Class A common stock and Class B common stock, as applicable, with respect to which Class C common stock is distributed in the Dividend should be apportioned between such share of Class A common stock or Class B common stock, on the one hand, and the Class C common stock received in the Dividend, on the other hand, in proportion to the fair market values of such shares on the date of the Dividend and (ii) if the shares of Class A common stock and Class B common stock with respect to which Class C common stock is distributed in the Dividend were held as capital assets, the holding period for each share of Class C common stock should include such shareholder’s holding period for the share of Class A common stock or Class B common stock, as applicable, with respect to which the Class C common stock is distributed. Gain or loss should be recognized on the subsequent disposition of shares of Class C common stock in a taxable transaction. Further, while we expect the Class C common stock not to constitute Section 306 stock, if the Class C common stock were determined to constitute Section 306 stock, a shareholder generally would be treated as realizing ordinary income as opposed to capital gain upon certain dispositions (including redemptions) of such Section 306 stock. All shareholders should consult their own tax advisers regarding the particular tax consequences to them of the receipt, ownership and disposition of shares of Class C common stock in light of their particular circumstances. Securities Act of 1933 The distribution of shares of Class C common stock as a stock dividend will not involve a "sale" of a security under the Securities Act of 1933, as amended (the "Securities Act"), or Rule 145 thereunder. Consequently, we are not required to register, and will not register, the Class C common stock distributed as part of the Dividend pursuant to the Securities Act, although we may register offerings of Class C common stock in the future. Because the Recapitalization and the Dividend do not constitute a "sale" of Class C common stock pursuant to the Securities Act, shareholders will not be deemed to have purchased such shares separately from the Class A common stock or Class B common stock to which such shares of Class C common stock relate pursuant to the Securities Act and Rule 144 thereunder. Shares of Class A common stock held at the time of the effectiveness of the Restated Charter and shares of Class C common stock (whether in respect of shares of Class A common stock or Class B common stock) received in the Dividend, other than any such shares held by "affiliates" of Meta within the meaning of the Securities Act, may be offered for sale and sold in the same manner as the Class A common stock prior to the Recapitalization without registration pursuant to the Securities Act (or in the case of equity awards, pursuant to registration statements that we will file under the Securities Act). Affiliates of Meta will continue to be subject to the restrictions specified in Rule 144 of the Securities Act. Nasdaq Criteria The shares of Class A common stock are currently traded on Nasdaq and will remain traded on Nasdaq following the Recapitalization and the Dividend. Application will be made to trade the shares of Class C common stock on Nasdaq following the Dividend. The listing of the shares of Class C common stock on Nasdaq is subject to Nasdaq’s approval of such listing applications and notices, which will be conditioned upon Meta’s satisfaction of certain listing requirements. We believe that we will be able to satisfy these listing requirements upon the payment of the Dividend. Potential Changes in Law or Regulation In prior years, bills have been introduced in Congress that, if enacted, would have prohibited the registration of common stock on a national securities exchange if such common stock was part of a class of securities that has no voting rights or carried disproportionate voting rights. Although these bills have not been acted upon by Congress, there can be no assurance that such a bill (or a modified version thereof) will not be introduced in Congress in the future. Legislation or other regulatory developments could make the shares of Class A common stock and Class C common stock ineligible for trading on Nasdaq or other national securities exchanges. We are unable to predict whether any such legislation or regulatory proposals will be adopted or whether they will have such effect. 38 Amendment to the 2025 Plan In connection with the Restated Charter, as discussed above, the Board and the Consenting Shareholders have approved the Equity Plan Amendment to provide that the shares reserved under the Plan Pool (as defined below) may be issued as either shares of Class A common stock or shares of Class C common stock. The Equity Plan Amendment does not increase the aggregate number of shares available for issuance under the 2025 Plan. Upon the effectiveness of the Equity Plan Amendment, the number of shares available for issuance pursuant to awards under the 2025 Plan shall be equal to the number of shares available for future grant and issuance under the 2025 Plan as of the Record Date, plus (i) any shares added to the 2025 Plan prior to the Charter Effective Date pursuant to the Evergreen Feature (as defined below), minus (ii) any shares that are subject to awards granted under the 2025 Plan between the Record Date and the Charter Effective Date, plus (iii) any shares subject to awards previously granted under the 2025 Plan that are outstanding as of the Charter Effective Date, plus (iv) any shares that are subject to awards granted under the 2025 Plan that cease to be subject to such award by forfeiture or otherwise, or that are used or withheld to pay the exercise price of an option or to satisfy the tax withholding obligations related to any award under the 2025 Plan, in each case between the Record Date and the Charter Effective Date, plus (v) any shares that are subject to awards granted under the 2012 Plan that cease to be subject to such award by forfeiture or otherwise after the Record Date, and plus (vi) any shares that are subject to awards under the 2012 Plan that are used or withheld to pay the exercise price of an option or to satisfy the tax withholding obligations related to any award after the Record Date (collectively, the "Plan Pool"). In addition, the number of shares reserved for grant and issuance under the 2025 Plan will increase automatically on January 1 of each of the calendar years during the remaining 10-year term of the 2025 Plan by a number of shares equal to the lesser of (i) 2.5% of the sum of the total outstanding shares of Class A common stock and Class C common stock as of the immediately preceding December 31 st or (ii) a number of shares determined by the Board. The Equity Plan Amendment will be effective upon the Charter Effective Date. The Equity Plan Amendment also provides that the Administrator may reprice underwater stock options and SARs without prior shareholder approval. Meta seeks to offer competitive compensation to attract and retain the best people. Meta employees' total compensation package generally includes market-competitive salary, bonuses or sales incentives, and equity offered in the form of Restricted Stock Units ("RSUs") at the time of hire and through annual RSU grants. Meta has also granted certain option awards to key employees. Our broad-based use of equity has been a key facet of our people practices and enables our employees to be owners of the Company and committed to our long-term success. Currently, nearly all full-time employees are awarded Meta equity. We believe that the 2025 Plan is a key element of the Board and management team’s strategy to align our employees’ interests with those of shareholders and motivate employees to help drive long-term growth and shareholder value. We also believe the 2025 Plan provides incentives to attract, retain, and motivate eligible persons whose present and potential contributions are important to the success of the Company, and any parents, subsidiaries and affiliates that exist now or in the future, by offering them an opportunity to participate in the Company’s future performance through the grant of awards. We generally expect to issue future equity awards in Class C common stock, in addition to continuing to permit equity awards of Class A common stock under the 2025 Plan. Administration The 2025 Plan is administered by our Compensation, Nominating & Governance Committee (the “Administrator”) or by the board of directors acting in place of our Compensation, Nominating & Governance Committee. The Administrator has the authority to construe and interpret the 2025 Plan, select participants and grant awards, and make all other determinations necessary or advisable for the administration of the 2025 Plan. The Administrator may delegate its authority under the 2025 Plan to a subcommittee consisting of one or more executive officers, subject to applicable law. The Administrator has delegated authority to grant certain awards under the 2025 Plan to the equity subcommittee of the Compensation, Nominating & Governance Committee. Eligibility The 2025 Plan provides for the grant of awards to our employees, directors, consultants, independent contractors, and advisors, provided the consultants, independent contractors, directors, and advisors render services not in connection with the offer and sale of securities in a capital-raising transaction. As of September 30, 2026, 66,775 employees (including each of our executive officers) and 11 non-employee directors were eligible to participate in the 2025 Plan. The basis for participation in the 2025 Plan is the Administrator's decision, in its sole discretion, that an award to an eligible participant will further the 2025 Plan's purposes of providing incentives to attract, retain, and motivate eligible persons whose present and potential contributions are important to our success and the success of our affiliates, by offering them an opportunity to participate in our future performance through the grant of awards. In exercising its discretion, the Administrator will consider the recommendations of management and the purposes of the 2025 Plan. Forms of Awards The following is a description of the types of awards permitted to be issued under the 2025 Plan. Restricted Stock Units . An RSU is an award that covers a number of shares of Class A or Class C common stock that may be settled upon vesting in cash, by the issuance of the underlying shares, or a combination of both. These awards are subject to forfeiture prior to vesting as a result of termination of employment or failure to achieve certain vesting conditions. Stock Options . The 2025 Plan provides for the grant of incentive stock options that qualify under Section 422 of the Code only to our employees. All awards other than incentive stock options, including awards of non-qualified stock options, may be granted to our employees, directors, consultants, independent contractors, and advisors, provided the consultants, independent contractors, and advisors render services not in connection with the offer and sale of securities in a capital-raising transaction. The exercise price of each stock option must be at least equal to the fair market value of the applicable underlying share on the date of grant. The exercise price of incentive stock options granted to 10% shareholders must be at least equal to 110% of that value. The maximum term of options granted under the 2025 Plan is ten years or, in the case of an incentive stock option granted to 10% shareholders, five years. 40 Restricted Stock Award . An RSA is an offer by us to sell shares of Class A or Class C common stock subject to restrictions. The price, if any, of an RSA will be determined by the Administrator. These awards are subject to forfeiture or repurchase prior to vesting as a result of termination of employment or failure to achieve certain vesting conditions. Stock Appreciation Rights . SARs provide for a payment, or payments, in cash or shares of Class A or Class C common stock, to the holder based upon the difference between the fair market value of the applicable underlying share on the date of exercise and the stated exercise price up to a maximum amount of cash or number of shares. These awards are subject to forfeiture prior to vesting as a result of termination of employment or failure to achieve certain vesting conditions. Performance Awards . A performance award is an award that covers an amount of cash or a number of shares of Class A or Class C common stock that may be settled upon achievement of the pre-established performance conditions in cash or by issuance of the underlying shares. These awards are subject to forfeiture prior to vesting as a result of termination of employment or failure to achieve the performance conditions. Stock Bonus Awards . A stock bonus award is an award that covers a number of shares of Class A or Class C common stock that may be granted as additional compensation for prior services or performance, in cash or by issuance of the underlying shares. Vesting conditions may or may not be applied to these awards. Additional Provisions Awards granted under the 2025 Plan may not be transferred in any manner other than by will or by the laws of descent and distribution, or as determined by the Administrator. Unless otherwise restricted by the Administrator, awards that are non-statutory stock options may be exercised during the lifetime of the optionee only by the optionee, the optionee's guardian or legal representative, or a family member of the optionee who has acquired the option by a permitted transfer. Awards that are incentive stock options may be exercised during the lifetime of the optionee only by the optionee or the optionee's guardian or legal representative. Stock options granted under the 2025 Plan generally may be exercised for a period of three months after the termination of the optionee's service to us, except in the case of death or permanent disability, in which case the options may be exercised for up to twelve months or six months, respectively, following termination of the optionee's service to us. Unless otherwise set forth in a participant's award agreement, vesting of RSUs, RSAs, SARs, performance awards, and stock bonus awards ceases on such participant's termination of service. Change of Control or Other Corporate Transactions If we experience a change in control transaction, outstanding awards, including any vesting provisions, may be assumed or substituted by the successor company. Outstanding awards that are not assumed or substituted will accelerate (unless otherwise determined by the Board) and will be exercisable for a period of time determined by the Administrator. The vesting of shares underlying outstanding awards to our non-employee directors will accelerate in full prior to the consummation of such transaction. If the intrinsic value of a stock option or SAR is equal to or less than zero, the Administrator may, in its sole discretion, provide for the cancellation of such awards without payment of any consideration therefor (for the avoidance of doubt, in the event of a change in control or other corporate transaction, the Administrator may, in its sole discretion, terminate any stock option or SAR for which the exercise price is equal to or exceeds the per share value of the consideration to be paid in the change in control or other corporate transaction without payment of consideration therefor). In the event there is a specified type of change in our capital structure without our receipt of consideration, such as a stock split, appropriate adjustments will be made to the number of shares reserved under the 2025 Plan, the maximum number of shares that can be granted in a calendar year, and the number of shares and exercise price, if applicable, of all outstanding awards under the 2025 Plan. Repricing Without prior shareholder approval the Administrator may (i) reprice stock options or SARs (where such repricing is a reduction in the exercise price of outstanding stock options or SARs, without the prior consent of the affected participant), and (ii) with the consent of the respective participants, pay cash or issue new awards in exchange for the surrender and cancellation of any, or all, outstanding awards. Cancellation or Clawback of Awards The Administrator may, to the extent permitted by applicable law and stock exchange rules or by any of our policies (including our Compensation Recoupment Policy and any other policy necessary to comply with applicable laws), cancel or require reimbursement of any awards granted, shares issued, or cash received upon the vesting, exercise, or settlement of 41 any awards granted to the participant or any shares of Class A or Class C common stock issued or cash received upon vesting, exercise, or settlement of any such awards or sale of applicable shares underlying such awards. Amendment and Termination The 2025 Plan will terminate on May 28, 2035, the tenth anniversary of its original effective date, unless it is terminated earlier by the Board. The Board may amend or terminate the 2025 Plan at any time, which may be without shareholder approval, unless required by applicable law or listing standards. Federal Income Tax Consequences The following is a brief summary of the federal income tax consequences applicable to awards granted under the 2025 Plan based on federal income tax laws in effect on the date of this Information Statement. This summary is not intended to be exhaustive and does not address all matters that may be relevant to a particular participant. The summary does not discuss the tax laws of any state, municipality, or foreign jurisdiction, or gift, estate, excise, payroll, or other tax laws other than federal income tax law. The following is not intended or written to be used, and cannot be used, for the purposes of avoiding taxpayer penalties. Because circumstances may vary, we advise all participants to consult their own tax advisors under all circumstances. Incentive Stock Options ("ISOs") . An optionee generally realizes no taxable income upon the grant or exercise of an ISO. However, the exercise of an ISO may result in an alternative minimum tax liability to the employee. With some exceptions, a disposition of shares purchased under an ISO within two years from the date of grant or within one year after exercise produces ordinary income to the optionee equal to the value of the shares at the time of exercise less the exercise price. The same amount is deductible by the Company as compensation, provided that the Company reports the income to the optionee. Any additional gain recognized in the disposition is treated as a capital gain for which the Company is not entitled to a deduction. However, if the optionee exercises an ISO and satisfies the holding period requirements, the Company may not deduct any amount in connection with the ISO. If a sale or disposition of shares acquired with the ISO occurs after the holding period, the employee will recognize long-term capital gain or loss at the time of sale equal to the difference between proceeds realized and the exercise price paid. In general, an ISO that is exercised by the optionee more than three months after termination of employment is treated as an NQSO (as defined below). ISOs are also treated as NQSOs to the extent that they first become exercisable by an individual in any calendar year for shares having a fair market value (determined as of the date of grant) in excess of $100,000. Non-Qualified Stock Options ("NQSOs") . An optionee generally has no taxable income at the time of grant of an NQSO but realizes income in connection with the exercise of the option in an amount equal to the excess (at the time of exercise) of the fair market value of shares acquired upon exercise over the exercise price. The same amount is deductible by the Company as compensation, provided that, in the case of an employee option, the Company reports the income to the employee. Upon a subsequent sale or exchange of the shares, any recognized gain or loss after the date of exercise is treated as capital gain or loss for which the Company is not entitled to a deduction. SARs . Generally, the recipient of a SAR will not recognize taxable income at the time the SAR is granted. If a participant receives the appreciation inherent in the SAR in cash, the cash will be taxed as ordinary income to the participant at the time it is received. If a participant receives the appreciation inherent in the SAR in shares, the spread between the then-current market value and the base price will be taxed as ordinary income to the participant at the time it is received. In general, there will be no federal income tax deduction allowed to the Company upon the grant or termination of SARs. However, upon the settlement of a SAR, the Company will be entitled to a deduction equal to the amount of ordinary income the recipient is required to recognize as a result of the settlement. Restricted Stock Awards . The recipient of an RSA will not recognize any taxable income for federal income tax purposes in the year of the award, provided that the shares are subject to restrictions (that is, they are nontransferable and subject to a substantial risk of forfeiture). However, the recipient may elect under Section 83(b) of the Code to recognize compensation income in the year of the award in an amount equal to the fair market value of the shares on the date of the award (less the purchase price, if any, paid for such shares), determined without regard to the restrictions. If a Section 83(b) election is made, the capital gain/loss holding period for such shares commences on the date of the award. Any further change in the value of the shares will be taxed as a capital gain or loss only if and when the shares are disposed of by the recipient. If the recipient does not make a Section 83(b) election, the fair market value of the shares on the date the restrictions lapse will be treated as compensation income to the recipient and will be taxable in the year the restrictions lapse, and the capital gain/loss holding period for such shares will also commence on such date. Restricted Stock Units . No income generally will be recognized upon the award of RSUs. The recipient of an RSU generally will be subject to tax at ordinary income rates on the market price of unrestricted shares on the date that such shares are transferred to the participant under the award (reduced by any amount paid, if any, by the participant for such RSUs), and the capital gain/loss holding period for such shares will also commence on such date. 42 Registration with the SEC Following the effective date of the Equity Plan Amendment, we intend to file with the SEC a registration statement on Form S-8 to reserve the shares of Class C common stock reserved for issuance under the 2025 Plan as a result of the Equity Plan Amendment. DIRECTOR COMPENSATION Non-employee Director Compensation Arrangements Our Board and shareholders have adopted a Director Compensation Policy that sets out the annual compensation for our non-employee directors and is designed to attract and retain highly qualified non-employee directors, which we believe is critical to our long-term success. Our Director Compensation Policy provides for cash retainers, excess meeting fees, initial equity grants upon joining our Board, and annual equity grants in amounts as approved by our Board. We believe our Director Compensation Policy allows us to appropriately compensate directors for: • the heavy workload resulting from the number of board and committee meetings each year, and the significant responsibilities of the various committees of our Board; • the external scrutiny faced by our non-employee directors, which is expected to be ongoing; and • service in demanding board leadership roles, such as our Lead Independent Director and committee chairs. On an annual basis, our compensation, nominating & governance committee reviews and makes recommendations to our Board regarding our non-employee director compensation arrangements, and our Board reviews and approves non-employee director compensation. As part of this review, our compensation, nominating & governance committee considers our directors' responsibilities and time commitment, as well as information regarding director compensation paid at peer companies, including an evaluation by its independent compensation consultant, Compensia, Inc. These awards will vest fully on the earlier of (i) May 15 of the following year or (ii) the date of our annual meeting of shareholders of the following year if the director does not stand for re-election or is not re-elected at such annual meeting, so long as the recipient is a director on such date. Initial Equity Grants New non-employee directors who join our Board between our annual meetings of shareholders are eligible to receive a one-time grant of RSUs at the time of their appointment with an initial equity value equal to the value of our annual grant of RSUs to our non-employee directors ($375,000), prorated for service from the date of their appointment through the next occurring May 15. These awards will vest fully on the earlier of (i) the next occurring May 15 or (ii) the date of our annual meeting of shareholders of the following year if the director does not stand for re-election or is not re-elected at such annual meeting, so long as the recipient is a director on such date. In addition, each new non-employee director who joins our Board is eligible to receive a one-time grant of RSUs at the time of their appointment with an initial equity value equal to $1 million. These awards will vest over approximately a four-year period in 16 equal quarterly installments. Annual Limit On Director Compensation Our Director Compensation Policy limits aggregate compensation (including both cash and equity-based compensation) payable to each non-employee director to $1,000,000 per year, except during such non-employee director's first year of service on our Board, in which case, such limit is $2,000,000 to account for the receipt of the initial equity grants to new non-employee directors ("Director Compensation Limit"). Director Security In light of the high level of scrutiny and elevated threat level faced by the Company and our directors, from time to time, we may provide personal security services to our non-employee directors and related tax gross-ups. As such services are non-compensatory in nature, they are not taken into consideration for any reason pursuant to our Director Compensation Policy, including when determining whether a non-employee director's compensation exceeds the Director Compensation Limit in any fiscal year. Director Deferred Compensation Plan Our Board has adopted the Meta Platforms, Inc. Deferred Compensation Plan for Non-Employee Directors (the "Deferred Compensation Plan"), pursuant to which our non-employee directors may elect to defer all or a portion of their cash fees (including annual retainers and meeting fees) and/or grants of RSUs. The deferred cash fees will be deferred into one or more investment funds offered under the Deferred Compensation Plan and the RSUs will be treated as deferred RSUs that continue to be settled in our Class A common stock. Amounts deferred under the Deferred Compensation Plan shall be paid or settled, at the election of the director, following either (i) the non-employee director’s separation from service or (ii) a specified date, and in either (i) a lump sum or (ii) up to 10 equal annual installments. For 2025, Mr. Arnold and Ambassador Kimmitt elected to defer eligible cash fees and RSUs granted in 2025, in each case pursuant to the Deferred Compensation Plan.
